| Lake Name | State / UT | Type / Origin | High-Yield Prelims Exam Triggers |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Chilika Lake** | **Odisha** (Puri, Khordha, Ganjam) | **Brackish coastal lagoon** | • **Largest brackish water lagoon in India** (fed by Daya River)  <br>• Designated **1st Indian Ramsar site (1981)** along with Keoladeo NP  <br>• Habitat of the endangered **Irrawaddy Dolphin**  <br>• Nalabana Island declared Bird Sanctuary |
| **Vembanad Lake** | **Kerala** (Kottayam, Alappuzha, Ernakulam) | **Brackish lagoon / backwater** | • **LONGEST LAKE IN INDIA** (96.5 km); largest in Kerala  <br>• Famous **Nehru Trophy Boat Race (Vallam Kali)** held on Punnamada Lake  <br>• Willingdon Island (Kochi Port) man-made in it  <br>• Kumarakom Bird Sanctuary on its eastern shore |
| **Sasthamkotta Lake** | **Kerala** (Kollam) | **Freshwater lake** | • Known as the **"Queen of Lakes"**; largest freshwater lake in Kerala  <br>• Contains unique larva *Cavaborus* which cleanses and purifies lake water |
| **Ashtamudi Lake** | **Kerala** (Kollam) | **Brackish estuarine lagoon** | • Eight-armed (*Ashta-mudi*) palm-shaped lake; gateway to Kerala backwaters; Ramsar site |
| **Kolleru Lake** | **Andhra Pradesh** (Eluru/Krishna) | **Freshwater lake** | • Located between **Krishna and Godavari deltas**; fed by Budameru and Tammileru streams  <br>• Atapaka Bird Sanctuary (pelican paradise); Ramsar site |
| **Pulicat Lake** | **Andhra Pradesh (96%) & Tamil Nadu (4%)** | **Brackish water coastal lagoon** | • **Second largest brackish lagoon in India**  <br>• Separated from Bay of Bengal by the barrier island of **Sriharikota** (ISRO Satish Dhawan Space Centre)  <br>• Famous annual **Flamingo Festival** |
| **Loktak Lake** | **Manipur** (Moirang, Bishnupur) | **Freshwater lake** | • **Largest freshwater lake in North-East India**  <br>• Famous for heterogeneous mass of vegetation and soil called **Phumdis**  <br>• Hosts **Keibul Lamjao National Park** — **WORLD'S ONLY FLOATING NATIONAL PARK**  <br>• Exclusive natural habitat of endangered **Sangai (Brow-antlered / Dancing deer)** |
| **Lonar Lake** | **Maharashtra** (Buldhana district) | **Meteorite Impact Crater Lake** | • Created by hyper-velocity meteorite impact during Pleistocene epoch in Deccan basalt  <br>• Highly saline and alkaline (pH ~10.5); turns pink due to haloarchaea microbes  <br>• Notified National Geo-heritage Monument; Ramsar site (2020) |
| **Wular Lake** | **Jammu & Kashmir** (Bandipora) | **Tectonic Freshwater Lake** | • **LARGEST FRESHWATER LAKE IN INDIA**  <br>• Formed by tectonic activity; fed and drained by **Jhelum River**  <br>• **Tulbul Navigation Project** (disputed with Pakistan) located at its mouth |
| **Dal Lake** | **Jammu & Kashmir** (Srinagar) | Freshwater flood-plain lake | • "Jewel in the crown of Kashmir"; famous for **Shikaras** and floating gardens (*Radh*) |
| **Sambhar Lake** | **Rajasthan** (Jaipur, Nagaur, Ajmer) | **Inland Saline Playa Lake** | • **LARGEST INLAND SALINE LAKE IN INDIA**; represents an endorheic depression  <br>• Produces ~9% of India's total salt production; Ramsar site |
| **Didwana, Pachpadra, Lunkaransar** | **Rajasthan** | Inland saline playas | • Pachpadra produces high-grade sodium chloride (98% purity) used locally |
| **Pushkar & Nakki Lake** | **Rajasthan** | Pushkar: Sacred holy lake; Nakki: Mount Abu | • Nakki is highest lake in Rajasthan; legend says dug by gods' nails (*nakh*) |
| **Pangong Tso** | **Ladakh** (4,225 m elevation) | Endorheic saltwater lake | • **World's highest saltwater lake**; ~1/3 lies in India and ~2/3 in Tibet (China) |
| **Tso Moriri** | **Ladakh** (Rupshu valley) | High-altitude brackish lake | • Breeding ground for endangered Black-necked Crane and Bar-headed Goose; Ramsar site |
| **Roopkund Lake** | **Uttarakhand** (Chamoli, 5,029 m) | Glacial tarn lake | • Known as the **"Mystery Lake" / "Skeleton Lake"**; hundreds of human skeletons found |
| **Bhimtal, Nainital, Naukuchiatal, Sattal** | **Uttarakhand** (Kumaon) | Tectonic / fault freshwater lakes | • **Bhimtal** is the largest lake in Kumaon with a central island; Naukuchiatal has 9 corners |
| **Suraj Tal & Chandra Tal** | **Himachal Pradesh** (Lahaul & Spiti) | High-altitude glacial lakes | • Chandra Tal (source of Chandra river) and Suraj Tal (source of Bhaga river); join to form Chenab |
| **Renuka Lake** | **Himachal Pradesh** (Sirmaur) | Freshwater wetland lake | • Largest natural lake in Himachal Pradesh; shaped like a reclining woman; Ramsar site |
| **Deepor Beel** | **Assam** (Kamrup / Guwahati) | Freshwater oxbow lake | • Former channel of Brahmaputra; vital staging site for migratory waterbirds; Ramsar site |
| **Rudrasagar Lake** | **Tripura** (Melaghar) | Freshwater lowland lake | • Historic **Neermahal Palace** situated in the center of the lake; Ramsar site |

## 16. Soils of India — ICAR Classification, Characteristics & Soil Health Master Card

### A. ICAR 8 Soil Groups (% Share & Leading Characteristics)
1. **Alluvial Soil (~43.4% of total area — Largest)**:
   * **Genesis**: Deposited by Indus, Ganga, Brahmaputra river systems and coastal waves.
   * **Nutrient Profile**: **Rich in Potash and Lime**; severely **deficient in Nitrogen, Phosphorus, and Humus**.
   * **Classification**:
     - **Khadar**: New alluvium deposited by annual floods; fine loamy texture, highly fertile; light color.
     - **Bhangar**: Older alluvium situated above flood levels; clayey texture, less fertile; contains calcareous nodules (**Kankar**).
     - **Bhabar**: Narrow porous pebble-bed at foot of Shiwaliks where rivers disappear underground.
     - **Terai**: Marshy, waterlogged damp tract south of Bhabar with tall elephant grass; re-emerging streams.
   * **Crops**: Wheat, Rice, Sugarcane, Jute, Oilseeds, Maize, Pulses.
2. **Red & Yellow Soil (~18.6% — 2nd Largest Group)**:
   * **Genesis**: Weathering of ancient crystalline igneous (granites, gneisses) and metamorphic rocks in low-rainfall areas.
   * **Color Rationale**: Red color due to wide diffusion of **Iron (Ferric oxides)**; appears yellow when hydrated.
   * **Nutrient Profile**: **Deficient in Nitrogen, Phosphorus, Lime, and Humus**.
   * **Regions & Crops**: Tamil Nadu, Southern Karnataka, Telangana, Odisha, Chotanagpur, MP; ideal for millets, pulses, groundnut, tobacco.
3. **Black Soil / Regur / Tropical Chernozem (~15.2%)**:
   * **Genesis**: Weathering of basaltic lava rocks of the **Deccan Trap (Cretaceous lava flows)**.
   * **Distinctive Features**: Highly argillaceous (clayey >50%); swells and becomes sticky when wet; shrinks and develops wide deep cracks when dry (**"self-ploughing" capacity**); exceptional moisture-retention capability.
   * **Color**: Black color attributed to **Titaniferous Magnetite** and iron compounds.
   * **Nutrient Profile**: **Rich in Lime, Iron, Magnesia, Alumina, and Potash**; severely **deficient in Phosphorus, Nitrogen, and Organic Matter**.
   * **Regions & Crops**: Maharashtra, Gujarat, Malwa (MP), Northern Karnataka, Telangana; **Ideal for Cotton (Black Cotton Soil), Soyabean, Sugarcane, Citrus fruits**.
4. **Laterite Soil (~3.7%)**:
   * **Genesis**: Formed under conditions of **high temperature and alternating heavy wet and dry seasons with intense tropical rainfall**.
   * **Process**: Intense **leaching (desilication)** washes away silica and lime, leaving behind residual oxides of **Iron and Aluminium (Bauxite)**.
   * **Properties**: Highly acidic (pH 4.5–5.5); hardens like brick upon exposure to atmosphere (Latin *Later* = brick).
   * **Nutrient Profile**: Poor in Nitrogen, Phosphorus, Potash, Lime, and Humus.
   * **Regions & Crops**: Summits of Western Ghats (Kerala, Karnataka, Maharashtra), Eastern Ghats, Meghalaya plateau, Rajmahal Hills; **Premier soil for Plantation Crops: Tea, Coffee, Rubber, Cashewnut, Coconut**.
5. **Arid / Desert Soil (~4.4%)**:
   * **Properties**: Sandy to gravelly texture, saline in nature; low moisture and very low humus; phosphate normal but nitrogen deficient; with canal irrigation (Indira Gandhi Canal) produces bumper crops of wheat, barley, cotton.
6. **Saline & Alkaline Soils (Reh, Kallar, Usar, Rakar, Thur, Chopan) (~1.3%)**:
   * **Genesis**: Excessive canal irrigation in poorly drained arid/semi-arid regions causing high capillary action that pulls soluble sodium, calcium, and magnesium salts to topsoil.
   * **Reclamation**: Application of **Gypsum (CaSO4·2H2O)** or Iron Pyrites to neutralize alkalinity; growing salt-tolerant green manure crops like **Dhaincha (*Sesbania*)**.
7. **Peaty and Marshy Soil (Kari) (~2%)**:
   * **Genesis**: Formed in humid regions with heavy rainfall and dense vegetation; high organic matter accumulation (40–50%).
   * **Properties**: Dark, heavy, highly acidic; found in **Kottayam and Alappuzha districts of Kerala (Kari lands)**, Sundarbans delta.
8. **Forest and Mountain Soil (~8.7%)**:
   * Immature loamy soil rich in humus from fallen leaf litter; acidic in higher alpine zones; terrace cultivation of apples, peaches, spices, tea.

## 17. Natural Vegetation & Forests of India (Champion & Seth / ISFR Key Indicators & Species)

### A. Core Forest Statistics (India State of Forest Report - ISFR)
* **Total Forest & Tree Cover**: **~24.62%** of geographical area (Forest Cover: ~21.71%; Tree Cover: ~2.91%).
* **National Forest Policy (1988) Target**: **33%** of total geographical area (60% in mountainous/hilly regions).
* **State with Largest Forest Cover by Area**: **Madhya Pradesh** (1st) > Arunachal Pradesh (2nd) > Chhattisgarh (3rd) > Odisha (4th) > Maharashtra (5th).
* **State with Highest Forest Cover as % of Total Area**: **Mizoram** (84.5%) > Arunachal Pradesh (79.3%) > Meghalaya (76%) > Manipur (74.3%).
* **UT with Highest Forest Cover %**: **Lakshadweep** (90.3%) and **Andaman & Nicobar Islands** (81.7%).

### B. Major Forest Types & Indicator Species (Match the Following Goldmine)

| Forest Type | Annual Rainfall & Climate | Typical Regions | Dominant Commercial Tree Species (Prelims Match) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Tropical Wet Evergreen** | **>250 cm**; Temp: 25–27°C; High humidity (>77%) | Western slopes of Western Ghats, NE Hills (Assam, Meghalaya), Andaman & Nicobar | • **Rosewood, Mahogany, Ebony, Aini, Cinchona (Quinine), Rubber, Wild Mango, Bamboo**  <br>• Dense multi-layered canopy; trees do not shed leaves simultaneously. |
| **Tropical Semi-Evergreen** | **200–250 cm** | Bordering wet evergreen belts in Western Ghats, Assam, Odisha | • **White Cedar, Hollock, Kadam, Kail, Champa** |
| **Tropical Moist Deciduous** | **100–200 cm**; distinct dry season | Eastern slopes of Western Ghats, Chotanagpur, foothills of Himalayas (Bhabar-Terai), Odisha, MP, UP | • **Teak (Sagan), Sal (Sakhu), Shisham, Sandalwood (Chandan), Mahua, Mulberry, Amla, Kusum, Semul, Bamboo**  <br>• Economically **most valuable timber forests of India**; shed leaves for 6–8 weeks in spring. |
| **Tropical Dry Deciduous** | **70–100 cm**; transitional between moist deciduous & thorn | Vast plains of UP, Bihar, MP, eastern Rajasthan, Deccan plateau | • **Tendu (Bidi wrapper), Palas / Dhak (*Flame of the Forest*), Amaltas, Bel, Khair, Axlewood (Dhaora)**  <br>• **MOST WIDESPREAD FOREST COVER IN INDIA (~41.8% of total forest area)**. |
| **Tropical Thorn & Scrub** | **<70 cm / <50 cm** | Rajasthan, SW Punjab, Haryana, Gujarat, rain-shadow leeward parts of Deccan | • **Acacia (Babool), Kikar, Ber, Wild Date Palm, Khair, Cacti, Euphorbia**  <br>• Deep roots, xerophytic thorns, fleshy stems to conserve water. |
| **Montane Subtropical / Temperate** | Altitude: **1,000 to 3,000 m** | Himalayas (J&K, HP, Uttarakhand, Sikkim) & Nilgiris/Anamalai (*Shola* forests) | • **1000–2000 m**: Broad-leaved **Oak and Chestnut**  <br>• **1500–3000 m**: Coniferous **Chir Pine** (resin & turpentine), **Deodar (*Cedrus deodara* — durable structural timber, western Himalayas), Blue Pine, Spruce, Silver Fir**  <br>• *Shola*: Stunted evergreen temperate grasslands/forests in Nilgiris and Anamalai. |
| **Alpine & Sub-Alpine** | Altitude: **>3,000 to 4,000 m** | High Himalayas above timber line up to snow line | • **Silver Fir, Juniper, Birch (Bhojpatra — ancient parchment bark), Rhododendron**  <br>• Alpine pastures called **Bugyals** in Uttarakhand and **Margs** in Kashmir (Gulmarg, Sonamarg). |
| **Littoral / Mangrove / Tidal** | Coastal saline estuaries, deltas, tidal creeks | Sundarbans (WB), Mahanadi delta (Bhitarkanika, Odisha), Godavari-Krishna deltas, Pichavaram (TN) | • **Sundari (*Heritiera fomes*), Goran, Kankra, Rhizophora, Sonneratia**  <br>• Halophytic adaptations: **Pneumatophores (breathing roots)**, stilt/prop roots, **vivipary** germination. |

## 18. Shifting Cultivation (Jhum) Regional & Global Names Master Match-Sheet

### A. Indian Regional Names for Shifting Cultivation
* **Jhum / Jhoom**: **North-Eastern India** (Assam, Meghalaya, Nagaland, Mizoram, Manipur, Arunachal Pradesh)
* **Podu / Dabi / Koman / Bhringa**: **Odisha** and Northern **Andhra Pradesh**
* **Bewar / Dahiya**: **Madhya Pradesh** (practiced by Baiga tribe in Bundelkhand/Baghelkhand)
* **Penda / Dipa**: **Bastar district (Chhattisgarh)** (practiced by Maria Gonds)
* **Valre / Waltre**: **South-Eastern Rajasthan** (Aravalli tribal belt)
* **Khil**: **Himalayan belt** (Himachal Pradesh, Uttarakhand)
* **Kuruwa / Khallu**: **Jharkhand** (Santhal Parganas)
* **Kumari**: **Western Ghats** (hilly tracts of Kerala and Karnataka)
* **Zabo**: **Nagaland** (indigenous forest-clearing + terraced pond water management)

### B. International Names for Shifting Cultivation (Direct Exam Matches)
* **Milpa**: **Mexico and Central America**
* **Ladang**: **Indonesia and Malaysia**
* **Roca**: **Brazil** (Amazon basin)
* **Conuco**: **Venezuela** (Orinoco basin)
* **Taungya**: **Myanmar (Burma)**
* **Chena**: **Sri Lanka**
* **Ray**: **Vietnam and Laos**
* **Tamrai**: **Thailand**
* **Caingin**: **Philippines**
* **Masole**: **Congo Basin (Central Africa)**
* **Chitemene**: **Zambia, Zimbabwe and Malawi**

## 19. Agricultural Revolutions, Commodity Boards & Premier National Research Institutes

### A. Agricultural Revolutions & Famous Pioneers
* **Green Revolution**: **Foodgrains (Wheat & Rice)** — Global Father: **Dr. Norman Borlaug** (Nobel Peace Prize 1970; semi-dwarf wheat at CIMMYT Mexico); Indian Father: **Dr. M.S. Swaminathan** (Bharat Ratna); Pantnagar University (1st agri university in India, 1960).
* **White Revolution (Operation Flood)**: **Milk & Dairy** — Father: **Dr. Verghese Kurien**; NDDB Anand, Gujarat (AMUL); launched in 1970.
* **Yellow Revolution**: **Oilseeds (Mustard & Sunflower)** — Pioneer: **Sam Pitroda** (Technology Mission on Oilseeds 1986).
* **Blue Revolution**: **Fish & Aquaculture** — Pioneers: **Dr. Hiralal Chaudhari** and **Dr. Arun Krishnan**.
* **Golden Revolution**: **Horticulture, Honey & Fruits** — Pioneer: **Nirpakh Tutej**.
* **Golden Fibre Revolution**: **Jute Production**.
* **Silver Revolution**: **Eggs & Poultry Production** — Pioneer: **Smt. Indira Gandhi**.
* **Silver Fibre Revolution**: **Cotton Production**.
* **Pink Revolution**: **Onion, Prawn / Shrimp & Pharmaceuticals** — Pioneer: **Durgesh Patel**.
* **Red Revolution**: **Meat and Tomato Production** — Pioneer: **Vishal Tewari**.
* **Grey Revolution**: **Chemical Fertilizers**.
* **Round Revolution**: **Potato Production**.
* **Brown Revolution**: **Leather, Cocoa & Non-Conventional Energy**.
* **Evergreen / Rainbow Revolution**: **Holistic sustainable agriculture** (coined by M.S. Swaminathan).

* **Coffee Board of India**: **Bengaluru** (Karnataka)
* **Rubber Board of India**: **Kottayam** (Kerala)

  - **Central Tuber Crops Research Institute (CTCRI)**: **Thiruvananthapuram** (Kerala)
  - **Central Tobacco Research Institute (CTRI)**: **Rajahmundry** (Andhra Pradesh)
  - **Central Sheep and Wool Research Institute (CSWRI)**: **Avikanagar** (Rajasthan)
  - **Central Institute for Research on Buffaloes (CIRB)**: **Hisar** (Haryana)

## 20. World Strategic Straits, Canals & Maritime Chokepoints Master Matrix

| Strait / Canal | Water Bodies Connected | Landmasses Separated | High-Yield Prelims Exam Triggers |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Strait of Malacca** | **Andaman Sea (Indian Ocean) & South China Sea (Pacific Ocean)** | **Malay Peninsula & Sumatra (Indonesia)** | • **World's busiest shipping chokepoint**; shortest sea route between Persian Gulf/India and China/Japan |
| **Strait of Hormuz** | **Persian Gulf & Gulf of Oman (Arabian Sea)** | **Iran & Oman / UAE (Musandam Peninsula)** | • **World's most critical oil transit chokepoint** (~20% of global petroleum passes through it) |
| **Bab-el-Mandeb** | **Red Sea & Gulf of Aden (Indian Ocean)** | **Yemen (Arabia/Asia) & Djibouti / Eritrea (Horn of Africa)** | • Known as the **"Gate of Tears"**; connects Indian Ocean shipping to the Suez Canal |
| **Suez Canal** | **Mediterranean Sea & Red Sea** | **Sinai Peninsula & Mainland Egypt (Africa)** | • Opened in **1869**; designed by French engineer **Ferdinand de Lesseps**  <br>• Eliminates passage around Cape of Good Hope; Port Said (north) and Port Tewfik/Suez (south) |
| **Strait of Gibraltar** | **Atlantic Ocean & Mediterranean Sea** | **Spain (Europe) & Morocco (Africa)** | • Known as the **"Key to the Mediterranean"** / ancient **"Pillars of Hercules"**; Rock of Gibraltar (UK) on north |
| **Bosporus (Bosphorus) Strait** | **Black Sea & Sea of Marmara** | **European Istanbul & Asian Istanbul (Turkey)** | • World's narrowest strait used for international navigation; separates Europe from Asia |
| **Dardanelles Strait** | **Sea of Marmara & Aegean Sea (Mediterranean)** | **Gallipoli Peninsula (Europe) & Asian Turkey** | • Historic naval battle of Gallipoli (WWI); Turkish Straits system regulated by Montreux Convention |
| **Bering Strait** | **Chukchi Sea (Arctic Ocean) & Bering Sea (Pacific Ocean)** | **Russia (Siberia/Asia) & Alaska (USA/North America)** | • **International Date Line (IDL)** passes directly through this strait |
| **Palk Strait** | **Bay of Bengal & Palk Bay / Gulf of Mannar** | **Tamil Nadu (India) & Jaffna Peninsula (Sri Lanka)** | • Named after Robert Palk (Governor of Madras) |
| **Strait of Dover** | **English Channel & North Sea** | **England (Great Britain) & France (Mainland Europe)** | • Narrowest part of English Channel (34 km); Channel Tunnel (*Chunnel*) runs beneath it |
| **Panama Canal** | **Atlantic Ocean (Caribbean Sea) & Pacific Ocean** | **North America & South America (Isthmus of Panama)** | • Opened in **1914**; 82 km long; uses 3 sets of freshwater locks (Gatun, Pedro Miguel, Miraflores) |
| **Kiel Canal** | **North Sea & Baltic Sea** | **Jutland Peninsula (Germany)** | • Bypasses the Danish straits; world's busiest artificial waterway by vessel count |
| **Sunda Strait** | **Java Sea & Indian Ocean** | **Java and Sumatra (Indonesia)** | • **Krakatoa** volcanic island sits directly inside this strait |
| **Lombok Strait** | **Java Sea & Indian Ocean** | **Bali and Lombok (Indonesia)** | • Deeper alternative to Malacca for supertankers; marks the **Wallace Line** biogeographical boundary |
| **Torres Strait** | **Arafura Sea & Coral Sea (Pacific Ocean)** | **Cape York (Australia) & Papua New Guinea** | • Shallow tropical strait filled with coral reefs |
| **Bass Strait** | **Tasman Sea & Southern Ocean / Indian Ocean** | **Victoria (Mainland Australia) & Tasmania** | • King Island and Flinders Island lie in it |
| **Cook Strait** | **Tasman Sea & South Pacific Ocean** | **North Island & South Island (New Zealand)** | • Connects capital Wellington with South Island |
| **Strait of Magellan** | **Atlantic Ocean & Pacific Ocean** | **Mainland South America (Chile) & Tierra del Fuego** | • Natural passage discovered by Ferdinand Magellan (1520) avoiding turbulent Drake Passage |
| **Drake Passage** | **South Atlantic Ocean & South Pacific Ocean** | **Cape Horn (South America) & South Shetland Islands (Antarctica)** | • Roughest open water body on Earth; connects Atlantic to Southern Ocean |
| **Florida Strait** | **Gulf of Mexico & Atlantic Ocean** | **Florida Keys (USA) & Cuba** | • Conveys the powerful warm Gulf Stream northward |

## 21. Major World Rivers, Drainage Features & Riverbank Cities Master Table

### A. World Landmark Rivers & Unique Geographic Traits

| River Name | Length & Basin | Source & Mouth | Landmark Exam Triggers & Geographic Superlatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Nile River** | **6,650 km** (Africa) | White Nile (Lake Victoria) + Blue Nile (Lake Tana) → **Mediterranean Sea** | • **LONGEST RIVER IN THE WORLD**  <br>• Confluence of White & Blue Nile at **Khartoum (Sudan)**  <br>• Forms classic **Arcuate Delta** in Egypt  <br>• **Aswan High Dam** creates **Lake Nasser**  <br>• Capitals on banks: **Cairo** (Egypt), **Khartoum** (Sudan) |
| **Amazon River** | **6,400 km** (South America) | Peruvian Andes (Nevado Mismi) → **Atlantic Ocean** | • **LARGEST RIVER IN THE WORLD BY WATER VOLUME & BASIN AREA** (~20% of world river discharge)  <br>• Flows across dense tropical rainforests (**Selvas** — "Lungs of the Earth")  <br>• Has **NO bridges** across its entire main stem  <br>• Spectacular tidal bore known as **Pororoca** |
| **Yangtze River (Chang Jiang)** | **6,300 km** (Asia / China) | Tibetan Plateau (Tangjula Mtns) → **East China Sea** (Shanghai) | • **LONGEST RIVER IN ASIA** (3rd longest in world); flows entirely in China  <br>• Hosts **Three Gorges Dam** (world's largest hydroelectric plant by capacity)  <br>• Cities: **Chongqing, Wuhan, Nanjing, Shanghai** |
| **Mississippi–Missouri** | **6,275 km** (North America) | Lake Itasca (Minnesota) → **Gulf of Mexico** | • Longest river system in North America; forms textbook **Bird's Foot Delta**  <br>• Cities: Minneapolis, St. Louis, Memphis, New Orleans |
| **Danube River** | **2,850 km** (Europe) | Black Forest (Germany) → **Black Sea** | • Flows through **10 countries** (more than any other river in the world)  <br>• Passes through **4 national capitals**: **Vienna** (Austria), **Bratislava** (Slovakia), **Budapest** (Hungary), **Belgrade** (Serbia) |
| **Rhine River** | **1,230 km** (Europe) | Swiss Alps (Lake Constance) → **North Sea** | • Flows through a **rift valley** between **Vosges Mountains (France)** and **Black Forest (Germany)**  <br>• **Busiest commercial inland waterway in Europe**; mouth at **Rotterdam Port** (Netherlands)  <br>• Basel, Strasbourg, Bonn, Cologne on its banks |
| **Congo River (Zaire)** | **4,700 km** (Central Africa) | Katanga Plateau → **Atlantic Ocean** | • **DEEPEST RIVER IN THE WORLD** (>220 m); 2nd largest discharge after Amazon  <br>• **CROSSES THE EQUATOR TWICE!**  <br>• Boyoma (Stanley) Falls and Livingstone Falls; **Kinshasa** and **Brazzaville** face each other across it |
| **Limpopo River** | **1,750 km** (Southern Africa) | Witwatersrand (South Africa) → **Indian Ocean** (Mozambique) | • **CROSSES THE TROPIC OF CAPRICORN TWICE!** |
| **Mahi River** | **583 km** (India) | Vindhya Range (MP) → **Gulf of Khambhat** | • **CROSSES THE TROPIC OF CANCER TWICE!** |
| **Volga River** | **3,530 km** (Russia / Europe) | Valdai Hills → **Caspian Sea** | • **LONGEST RIVER IN EUROPE**; internal drainage into landlocked Caspian Sea; Volgograd (Stalingrad) on its bank |
| **Amur River (Heilong Jiang)** | **2,824 km** (Asia) | Confluence of Shilka & Argun → **Sea of Okhotsk** | • Forms the natural international border between **Russia and China** for >1,600 km |
| **Mekong River** | **4,350 km** (Southeast Asia) | Tibetan Plateau → **South China Sea** | • Known as the "Danube of the East"; flows through 6 countries: China, Myanmar, Laos, Thailand, Cambodia, Vietnam; **Vientiane** and **Phnom Penh** on banks |
| **St. Lawrence River** | **1,197 km** (North America) | Lake Ontario → **Gulf of St. Lawrence (Atlantic)** | • Natural outlet of the Great Lakes; forms part of USA–Canada international border; Montreal and Quebec City on its banks |

* **Paris**: **Seine** (France)
* **Rome**: **Tiber** (Italy)
* **Berlin**: **Spree** (Germany)
* **Vienna, Budapest, Belgrade**: **Danube** (Austria, Hungary, Serbia)

* **New Orleans**: **Mississippi** (USA)

* **Bangkok**: **Chao Phraya** (Thailand)

* **Karachi**: **Indus** (Pakistan)

## 22. World Lakes, Inland Seas & Salinity Master Matrix

| Lake Name | Location / Countries Bordering | Key Geographic Superlative | High-Yield Prelims Exam Triggers |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Caspian Sea** | Bordered by **5 Countries (mnemonic: TARIK)**:  <br>**T**urkmenistan, **A**zerbaijan, **R**ussia, **I**ran, **K**azakhstan | **LARGEST LAKE / INLAND WATER BODY IN THE WORLD** | • Surface area ~371,000 km²; slightly saline (~1.2%); largest endorheic basin  <br>• Baku (capital of Azerbaijan) situated on its western shore  <br>• Fed by Volga, Ural, and Kura rivers; has NO natural outlet |
| **Lake Superior** | **USA & Canada border** (Minnesota, Wisconsin, Michigan, Ontario) | **LARGEST FRESHWATER LAKE IN THE WORLD BY SURFACE AREA** | • Surface area ~82,100 km²; part of North American Great Lakes  <br>• Connected to Lake Huron by St. Mary's River and **Soo Locks** |
| **Lake Baikal** | **Russia** (Siberia — Irkutsk & Buryatia) | **DEEPEST LAKE IN THE WORLD** (1,642 m) | • **Oldest freshwater lake in the world** (25–30 million years)  <br>• **Largest lake by volume of unfrozen freshwater (~20% of world's surface freshwater)**  <br>• Drained by only ONE river: the **Angara River** (tributary of Yenisey); UNESCO WHS |
| **Lake Tanganyika** | Shared by **Tanzania, DR Congo, Burundi, Zambia** | **LONGEST FRESHWATER LAKE IN THE WORLD** (673 km) | • 2nd deepest lake in the world (1,470 m); situated in East African Great Rift Valley |
| **Lake Victoria** | Shared by **Tanzania (49%), Uganda (45%), Kenya (6%)** | **LARGEST TROPICAL LAKE IN THE WORLD** | • 2nd largest freshwater lake by surface area (68,800 km²)  <br>• **Equator passes directly through it**  <br>• Primary reservoir and source of the **White Nile River** |
| **Lake Titicaca** | **Andes Mountains** (Border of **Peru and Bolivia**) | **HIGHEST COMMERCIALLY NAVIGABLE LAKE IN THE WORLD** | • Elevation: **3,812 m** above sea level; high alpine lake  <br>• Indigenous Uros people live on artificial floating islands made of **Totora reeds** |
| **Dead Sea** | Bordered by **Jordan, Israel, and West Bank** | **LOWEST WATER BODY ON EARTH'S LAND SURFACE** | • Surface elevation: **~430 m below sea level**  <br>• Hypersaline terminal lake (~34% salinity — 10x oceans); high density allows humans to float effortlessly  <br>• Fed by Jordan River |
| **Lake Van** | **Eastern Turkey** (Anatolia) | **HIGHEST SALINITY AMONG MAJOR WATER BODIES (~330‰ / 33%)** | • Saline soda lake with no outlet; water does not freeze even at high elevation |
| **Lake Assal** | **Djibouti** (Afar Depression, Horn of Africa) | Hypersaline lake (~348‰ salinity) | • Lowest point in Africa (-155 m below sea level); 2nd highest salinity on Earth |
| **Lake Maracaibo** | **Venezuela** (South America) | Largest lake in South America | • Connected to Gulf of Venezuela by Tablazo Strait; rich petroleum basin; famous for **Catatumbo Lightning** |
| **Aral Sea** | Border of **Kazakhstan and Uzbekistan** | Catastrophic environmental shrinkage | • Formerly 4th largest lake; shrunken to <10% original size due to Soviet diversion of **Amu Darya and Syr Darya** rivers for cotton fields |
| **Lake Balkhash** | **Kazakhstan** (Central Asia) | Unique half-fresh, half-saline lake | • Western part is fresh water (fed by Ili River), while eastern part is saline water |
| **Lake Chad** | Shared by **Chad, Cameroon, Niger, Nigeria** | Endorheic Sahelian lake | • Crucial wetland in the Sahel; shrunken by >90% since 1960s |

### The Great Lakes of North America (Strict West-to-East Chronology)
* **Superior → Michigan → Huron → Erie → Ontario** (Mnemonic: **SMHEO** or **HOMES**).
* **Lake Michigan** is the **ONLY ONE of the 5 Great Lakes situated entirely within the United States** (the other four are shared between USA and Canada).
* **Niagara Falls** is located on the Niagara River between **Lake Erie and Lake Ontario**!
* **Welland Canal** bypasses Niagara Falls to connect Lake Erie and Lake Ontario for commercial shipping.

## 23. World Major Grasslands & Deserts Master Cheat-Sheet

### A. Grasslands of the World (Tropical Savannas vs Temperate Steppes)

| Grassland Name | Type (Temperate / Tropical) | Geographic Region & Countries | High-Yield Prelims Exam Triggers |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Prairies** | **Temperate** (Mid-latitude) | **North America (USA & Canada)** | • Known as the **"Granary of the World"** (massive commercial wheat belt)  <br>• Warm katabatic wind **Chinook** ("Snow Eater") melts winter snow on eastern slopes of Rockies. |
| **Steppes** | **Temperate** (Mid-latitude) | **Eurasia** (Ukraine, Southern Russia to Central Asia & Mongolia) | • Extensive temperate grassland plain; dark fertile **Chernozem** soils; premier wheat & grain farming. |
| **Pampas** | **Temperate** (Mid-latitude) | **South America (Argentina & Uruguay)** | • Fertile alluvial soils; rich in nutritious **Alfalfa** grass; extensive cattle ranching (**Estancias**) and cowboys (**Gauchos**); wheat cultivation. |
| **Veld / Veldt** | **Temperate** (Mid-latitude) | **South Africa** (High Veld & Bush Veld plateau) | • Renowned for rearing **Merino sheep** for high-grade wool and maize cultivation (Maize Triangle). |
| **Downs** | **Temperate** (Mid-latitude) | **Australia** (Murray–Darling River Basin) | • Rolling grassland plains; premier sheep-farming stations for wool and wheat growing. |
| **Canterbury Plains** | **Temperate** (Mid-latitude) | **New Zealand** (South Island) | • Rich pastoral plains for sheep rearing and dairy farming. |
| **Puszta** | **Temperate** (Mid-latitude) | **Hungary** (Danube River Basin) | • Traditional Hungarian horse-breeding and grain plain. |
| **Savanna / Sudan Type** | **Tropical** | **East & Central Africa** (Kenya, Tanzania, Sudan) | • Characterized by tall coarse grasses (elephant grass) and scattered umbrella-acacia / baobab trees  <br>• Known as **"Big Game Country"** (abundant herbivore & carnivore diversity); Serengeti; Masai pastoralists. |
| **Llanos** | **Tropical** | **Venezuela and Colombia** (Orinoco River Basin) | • Tropical savanna grassland with distinct wet and dry seasons; extensive cattle ranching. |
| **Campos** | **Tropical** | **Brazilian Highlands (Brazil)** | • Subtropical / tropical savanna plateau grasslands south of the Amazon forest. |
| **Cerrado** | **Tropical** | **Central Brazil** (Mato Grosso) | • Vast tropical savanna ecoregion; converted into world's largest soyabean and cattle production frontier. |
| **Gran Chaco** | **Tropical / Semi-arid** | **Paraguay, Bolivia, Northern Argentina** | • Scrub woodland and savanna; Quebracho tree (*axe-breaker*) source of tannin. |

### B. Major Deserts of the World & Origin Factors

| Desert Name | Location & Continents | Type & Origin Factors | Unique Exam Superlative / Highlights |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Sahara Desert** | **North Africa** (11 countries) | **Subtropical Hot Desert** | • **LARGEST HOT DESERT ON EARTH** (~9.2 million km²); covers ~30% of Africa; Ergs (sand seas), Regs (stone plains), Hamadas (rock plateaus). |
| **Atacama Desert** | **Northern Chile & Southern Peru** | **Coastal Hyper-Arid Desert** | • **DRIEST NON-POLAR DESERT ON EARTH**  <br>• Formed by rain-shadow of high Andes + cold desiccating **Humboldt (Peru) Current**  <br>• Vast deposits of **Sodium Nitrate (Caliche)** and world-class copper mines. |
| **Namib Desert** | **Namibia** (South-West Africa) | **Coastal Fog Desert** | • Formed by the cold **Benguela Current** offshore; ancient red sand dunes; home to ancient living fossil plant *Welwitschia mirabilis*. |
| **Kalahari Desert** | **Botswana, Namibia, South Africa** | **Semi-Arid Sandy Basin** | • Inhabited by the indigenous **San (Bushmen)** hunter-gatherers; vast red sand dunes (*Kgalagadi*). |
| **Australian Deserts** | **Central & Western Australia** (Great Victoria, Great Sandy, Gibson, Simpson) | **Subtropical Hot / Trade Wind Desert** | • Known as the **"Red Centre" / "Outback"**; formed by subtropical high-pressure belt + cold **West Australian Current**; Great Victoria is largest. |
| **Gobi Desert** | **Southern Mongolia & Northern China** | **Cold Continental Rain-Shadow Desert** | • Located in the rain-shadow of the Himalayas; extreme winter cold (-40°C); home to indigenous two-humped **Bactrian camels**. |
| **Taklamakan Desert** | **Xinjiang, Northwest China** (Tarim Basin) | **Cold Desert** | • Bounded by Tian Shan and Kunlun mountains; known as the "Sea of Death" / "Place of No Return". |
| **Rub' al Khali** | **Southern Arabian Peninsula** (Saudi Arabia, Oman, UAE, Yemen) | **Continuous Sand Desert (Erg)** | • Known as the **"Empty Quarter"**; **LARGEST CONTIGUOUS SAND DESERT IN THE WORLD** (~650,000 km²). |
| **Patagonian Desert** | **Southern Argentina** (South America) | **Cold Winter Rain-Shadow Desert** | • Rain-shadow of the Southern Andes; influenced by the cold **Falkland Current**; gravelly plateau. |
| **Dasht-e Kavir & Dasht-e Lut** | **Iran** (Central Plateau) | **Salt Deserts (Playa)** | • *Kavir* = great salt swamp; *Lut* recorded world's highest satellite land skin temperature (>70.7°C). |
| **Mojave & Sonoran Deserts** | **Southwestern USA (California, Arizona) & Mexico** | **Subtropical Hot Desert** | • Contains **Death Valley** (**Badwater Basin, -86 m** — lowest point in North America; hottest recorded air temp 56.7°C); giant **Saguaro cactus**. |

## 24. World Volcanoes, Oceanic Trenches & Landmark Islands

### A. Famous Volcanoes of the World (Direct Match Sheet)

| Volcano Name | Country / Geographic Location | Type / Activity | High-Yield Prelims Exam Triggers |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Mount Stromboli** | **Lipari Islands / Sicily, Italy** (Tyrrhenian Sea) | Active Stratovolcano | • Known as the **"LIGHTHOUSE OF THE MEDITERRANEAN"** due to continuous glowing eruptions visible for miles. |
| **Mount Etna** | **Sicily, Italy** | Active Stratovolcano | • **Highest active volcano in Europe** (~3,357 m); continuously active; UNESCO World Heritage Site. |
| **Mount Vesuvius** | **Gulf of Naples, Italy** | Active / Dormant Stratovolcano | • Catastrophic Plinian eruption in **79 AD** completely buried and preserved the Roman cities of **Pompeii and Herculaneum**. |
| **Mauna Loa & Kilauea** | **Big Island of Hawaii, USA** (Pacific Ocean) | Active Shield Volcanoes | • **Mauna Loa** is the **world's largest subaerial shield volcano** by volume and area  <br>• **Kilauea** is among the world's most continuously active volcanoes; fluid basaltic lava flows. |
| **Cotopaxi** | **Andes Mountains, Ecuador** (South America) | Active Stratovolcano | • One of the world's highest active volcanoes (5,897 m); textbook symmetrical snow-capped cone. |
| **Ojos del Salado** | **Andes Mountains (Border of Chile & Argentina)** | Active Stratovolcano | • **HIGHEST ACTIVE VOLCANO ON EARTH (6,893 m)**; 2nd highest peak in Western Hemisphere after Mount Aconcagua. |
| **Mount Kilimanjaro** | **Tanzania** (East Africa) | Dormant Stratovolcano | • **HIGHEST PEAK IN AFRICA (5,895 m)**; freestanding volcanic mountain with 3 volcanic cones: Kibo, Mawenzi, Shira; permanent snowcap near Equator. |
| **Krakatoa (Krakatau)** | **Sunda Strait (Between Java and Sumatra, Indonesia)** | Active Caldera Volcano | • Cataclysmic eruption in **1883** generated massive tsunamis and shockwaves heard 4,800 km away; modern growing cone is **Anak Krakatau**. |
| **Mount Merapi & Mount Sinabung** | **Java & Sumatra, Indonesia** | Highly Active Stratovolcanoes | • Merapi (*Mountain of Fire*) is the most active volcano in Indonesia; deadly pyroclastic density currents. |
| **Mount Pinatubo & Mayon** | **Luzon, Philippines** | Active Stratovolcanoes | • Pinatubo's massive **1991 eruption** injected huge SO2 aerosol clouds causing global temporary cooling of ~0.5°C  <br>• **Mayon** is celebrated for having the **world's most perfectly symmetrical volcanic cone**. |
| **Mount Fuji (Fujisan)** | **Honshu Island, Japan** | Dormant Stratovolcano | • Highest peak in Japan (3,776 m); sacred mountain; last erupted in 1707 (Hoei eruption). |
| **Mount Erebus** | **Ross Island, Antarctica** | Active Stratovolcano | • **SOUTHERNMOST ACTIVE VOLCANO ON EARTH**; features a long-lived persistent phonolitic lava lake. |
| **Barren Island** | **Andaman Sea, India** (~135 km NE of Port Blair) | **Active Volcano** | • **THE ONLY CONFIRMED ACTIVE VOLCANO IN SOUTH ASIA / INDIA**; erupted in 1991, 2005, and continues sporadic activity. |
| **Narcondam Island** | **Andaman Sea, India** | **Dormant Volcanic Island** | • Extinct/dormant andesite volcano; exclusive habitat of the endemic, endangered Narcondam Hornbill. |

### B. Deepest Oceanic Trenches of the World
* **Mariana Trench (Challenger Deep)**: **10,994 m** (Western Pacific Ocean near Guam) — **DEEPEST POINT ON PLANET EARTH**.
* **Tonga Trench (Horizon Deep)**: **10,882 m** (Southwest Pacific Ocean) — 2nd deepest trench on Earth.
* **Kuril–Kamchatka Trench**: **10,542 m** (Northwest Pacific Ocean).
* **Philippine Trench (Galathea Deep)**: **10,540 m** (Western Pacific Ocean).
* **Puerto Rico Trench (Milwaukee Deep)**: **8,376 m** (Atlantic Ocean) — **DEEPEST POINT IN THE ATLANTIC OCEAN**.
* **Java / Sunda Trench**: **7,450 m** (Eastern Indian Ocean off Sumatra/Java) — **DEEPEST POINT IN THE INDIAN OCEAN**.
* **Factorian Deep (South Sandwich Trench)**: **7,434 m** (Southern Ocean) — **DEEPEST POINT IN THE SOUTHERN OCEAN**.
* **Molloy Deep**: **5,550 m** (Fram Strait, Arctic Ocean) — **DEEPEST POINT IN THE ARCTIC OCEAN**.

## 25. World Major Industrial Regions & Specialised Manufacturing / Mining Centres

| Region / City | Country | Primary Manufacturing / Mineral Specialization | High-Yield Prelims Exam Nickname / Triggers |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Ruhr Valley & Saar Basin** | **Germany** | **Coal mining, Iron and Steel, Heavy Engineering** | • Known as the **"Ruhrgebiet" / Industrial Heart of Europe**; Duisburg (world's largest inland port). |
| **Pittsburgh** | **Pennsylvania, USA** | **Iron and Steel Industry** | • Celebrated as the **"STEEL CITY OF THE WORLD"**; confluence of Allegheny and Monongahela rivers. |
| **Detroit** | **Michigan, USA** | **Automobile Manufacturing** | • Celebrated as the **"AUTOMOBILE CAPITAL OF THE WORLD"** (Headquarters of Ford, GM, Chrysler). |
| **Birmingham** | **West Midlands, England, UK** | **Iron, Steel, Heavy Metal Hardware** | • Historic heart of the Industrial Revolution; central to the English **"Black Country"**. |
| **Manchester & Lancashire** | **North-West England, UK** | **Cotton Textile Manufacturing** | • World's premier historic cotton textile spinning cluster due to humid oceanic climate. |
| **Katanga (Shaba) & Copperbelt** | **DR Congo & Zambia** (Central Africa) | **Copper and Cobalt Extraction** | • World's richest mineral belt for battery-grade **Cobalt and high-grade Copper**. |
| **Witwatersrand ("The Rand")** | **Johannesburg, South Africa** | **Gold Mining** | • **World's largest gold-bearing reef formation**; produced ~40% of all gold mined in human history. |
| **Kimberley** | **Northern Cape, South Africa** | **Diamond Mining** | • Historic diamond capital; the **"Big Hole"**; origin of the geological term **Kimberlite pipe**. |
| **Baku** | **Azerbaijan** (Caspian Sea) | **Petroleum Extraction & Refining** | • Historic birthplace of modern commercial oil extraction; Nobel Brothers and Rothschild oil legacy. |
| **Krivoy Rog & Donbass (Donets Basin)** | **Ukraine** | **Iron Ore & Coking Coal** | • Krivoy Rog = massive iron-ore basin; Donbass = premier historic coking coalfield. |
| **Magnitogorsk & Kuznetsk (Kuzbass)** | **Russia** (Urals & Siberia) | **Iron & Steel Combine** | • Magnitogorsk built around Magnetic Mountain (iron ore); Kuznetsk is a giant Siberian coal basin. |
| **Upper Silesia** | **Poland** | **Coal Mining, Zinc & Lead, Heavy Industry** | • Industrial core of Central Europe around Katowice. |
| **Chuquicamata** | **Antofagasta, Atacama Desert, Chile** | **Copper Mining** | • **World's largest open-pit copper mine by volume** and second deepest open-pit mine on Earth. |
| **Potosí (Cerro Rico)** | **Bolivia** (Andes Mountains) | **Silver Mining** | • Historic "Rich Mountain" that bankrolled the Spanish Empire with vast silver wealth for centuries. |
| **Kalgoorlie & Coolgardie** | **Western Australia** | **Gold Mining** | • Historic Australian desert gold rush of 1890s; famous **"Golden Mile"** open-pit Super Pit gold mine. |
| **Sudbury Basin** | **Ontario, Canada** | **Nickel, Copper, Platinum Group Metals** | • Ancient 1.85-billion-year-old meteorite impact basin; premier global source of nickel. |

### Famous Indian Industrial Nicknames & City Specializations
* **Detroit of India**: **Chennai** (Tamil Nadu — automobile manufacturing hub of South Asia).
* **Manchester of India**: **Ahmedabad** (Gujarat — premier cotton textile hub).
* **Manchester of North India**: **Kanpur** (Uttar Pradesh — historic cotton mills and leather industry).
* **Manchester of South India**: **Coimbatore** (Tamil Nadu — cotton spinning mills).
* **Cottonopolis of India**: **Mumbai** (Maharashtra — historic concentration of textile mills).
* **Silicon Valley of India / Electronic City**: **Bengaluru** (Karnataka — premier IT & aerospace cluster).
* **Pittsburgh of India / Steel City**: **Jamshedpur / Tatanagar** (Jharkhand — TISCO established 1907 at Sakchi, confluence of Subarnarekha and Kharkai rivers).
* **Brass City (*Peetal Nagari*)**: **Moradabad** (Uttar Pradesh — world-renowned brass handicraft industry).
* **Glass City / Bangle City (*Suhag Nagari*)**: **Firozabad** (Uttar Pradesh — glass blowing and bangles).
* **Leather City of the World**: **Kanpur** (Uttar Pradesh).
* **Perfume Capital of India (*Itra Nagari*)**: **Kannauj** (Uttar Pradesh).
* **Weaver City / City of Handlooms**: **Panipat** (Haryana).
* **City of Locks**: **Aligarh** (Uttar Pradesh).
* **Sports Goods Hub**: **Meerut** (Uttar Pradesh) and **Jalandhar** (Punjab).

<div id="subject-06-environment-and-ecology" class="subject-banner">
<span class="badge">Subject 06 · Environment & Ecology</span>
<h1>SUBJECT 06: ENVIRONMENT & ECOLOGY</h1>
<h2>पर्यावरण एवं पारिस्थितिकी — दैनिक उच्च-प्राथमिकता तथ्य</h2>
<p>Environmental Legislation & Acts, Wildlife Projects, Biosphere Reserves, Ramsar Wetlands in UP & UK, International Conventions (UNFCCC, CBD, Ramsar), and IUCN Red List Status.</p>
</div>

## 1. Core Environmental Legislation in India (Exact Years & Articles)

| Legislation / Policy Name | Year of Enactment | Constitutional Backing / Key Mandate | Landmark Institutional Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Indian Forest Act** | **1927** (earlier 1865, 1878) | Consolidated forest laws; categorized into **Reserved, Protected, and Village Forests** | State Forest Departments |
| **Wildlife (Protection) Act (WPA)** | **1972** (9 Sept 1972) | Framework for protecting wild animals, birds, and plants; **6 original schedules restructured into 4 schedules in 2022 Amendment** | National Board for Wildlife (NBWL, chaired by PM); NTCA; Wildlife Crime Control Bureau (WCCB) |
| **Water (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act** | **1974** | Prevention and abatement of water pollution; maintains wholesomeness of water | Established **Central Pollution Control Board (CPCB)** & State PCBs |
| **Forest (Conservation) Act (FCA)** | **1980** (25 Oct 1980) | Prohibits diversion of forest land for non-forest purposes without prior central approval | Forest clearance mechanism |
| **Air (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act** | **1981** | Prevention, control, and abatement of air pollution; **Noise pollution included as an air pollutant by 1987 Amendment** | Empowered CPCB and SPCBs to set ambient air standards |
| **Environment (Protection) Act (EPA)** | **1986** (23 May 1986) | **Umbrella legislation** enacted under **Article 253** in the wake of the **Bhopal Gas Tragedy (Dec 1984)** | Empowered Union Govt to coordinate state actions, set emission norms; CRZ notifications; EIA rules |
| **Public Liability Insurance Act** | **1991** | Mandatory public insurance for providing immediate relief to victims of accidents occurring while handling hazardous substances | Environment Relief Fund |
| **National Environment Tribunal Act** | **1995** | Strict liability for damages arising out of any accident while handling hazardous substances | Precursor to NGT |
| **Biological Diversity Act (BDA)** | **2002** | Realized commitments under CBD (1992); 3-tier structure for conservation and equitable benefit sharing | **National Biodiversity Authority (NBA, Chennai)**, State Biodiversity Boards (SBB), Biodiversity Management Committees (BMC) |
| **Forest Rights Act (FRA)** | **2006** | Scheduled Tribes and Other Traditional Forest Dwellers (Recognition of Forest Rights) Act; vests forest land rights | Gram Sabha is the statutory authority to initiate rights recognition process |
| **National Green Tribunal (NGT) Act** | **2010** (18 Oct 2010) | Created dedicated environmental tribunal under **Article 21 (Right to a healthy environment)**; mandated disposal within **6 months** | **Principal Bench: New Delhi**; 4 Regional Benches: **Bhopal, Pune, Kolkata, Chennai** |
| **CAMPA Act** | **2016** | Compensatory Afforestation Fund Act; manages funds collected for diversion of forest land (90% to State, 10% to Centre) | National & State Compensatory Afforestation Funds |

---

## 2. Environmental Movements in India (Pioneers & Locations)

| Movement | Year & Place | Key Leaders / Activists | Core Environmental Objective / Strategy |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Bishnoi Movement** | **1730**, Khejarli village (Jodhpur, Rajasthan) | **Amrita Devi Bishnoi** | King Abhay Singh ordered felling of **Khejri trees** (*Prosopis cineraria*); Amrita Devi and her 3 daughters hugged trees; **363 Bishnois sacrificed their lives**; Amrita Devi Bishnoi Wildlife Protection Award instituted in memory. |
| **Chipko Movement** | **1973**, Reni village, Chamoli (**Uttarakhand**) | **Sunderlal Bahuguna**, **Chandi Prasad Bhatt** (Ramon Magsaysay awardee), **Gaura Devi** (*Chipko Mother*) | Protest against commercial logging of ash trees in Alaknanda basin; villagers hugged trees to prevent felling; coined slogan: *"Ecology is permanent economy"*; led to 15-year ban on green felling in UP/UK hills. |
| **Appiko Movement** | **1983**, Salkani, Uttara Kannada (Karnataka) | **Pandurang Hegde** | Southern version of Chipko; hugged trees in Western Ghats to save tropical forests from commercial monoculture plantations. |
| **Silent Valley Movement** | **1978**, Palakkad (Kerala) | Kerala Sastra Sahitya Parishad (**KSSP**), Romulus Whitaker | Protest against proposed **hydroelectric project on Kunthipuzha River** that would submerge the pristine moist evergreen rainforest habitat of the endangered **Lion-Tailed Macaque**; project scrapped; declared Silent Valley National Park (1984). |
| **Narmada Bachao Andolan (NBA)** | **1985**, Narmada Valley (Gujarat, MP, Maha) | **Medha Patkar**, **Baba Amte** | Mass movement against large dams on Narmada (particularly **Sardar Sarovar Dam**); demanded comprehensive rehabilitation, environmental impact assessment, and rights of displaced tribal villagers. |
| **Jungle Bachao Andolan** | **1982**, Singhbhum (Bihar / Jharkhand) | Local tribal communities | Tribals agitated against government scheme to replace indigenous, multipurpose **Sal forests** with commercially profitable **Teak (Sagwan)** plantations; known as "Greed Game Political Populism". |
| **Tehri Dam Protest** | 1980s–1990s, Tehri (**Uttarakhand**) | **Sunderlal Bahuguna** | Agitation against the construction of the mega Tehri Dam on the seismic fault lines of Bhagirathi River; multiple hunger strikes. |

|---|---|---|
| **Botanical Survey of India (BSI)** | **Kolkata** (West Bengal) | **1890**; survey and conservation of plant resources of India |
| **Zoological Survey of India (ZSI)** | **Kolkata** (West Bengal) | **1916**; survey, exploration, and taxonomic research on animal diversity |

| **Wildlife Institute of India (WII)** | **Dehradun (Uttarakhand)** | **1982**; premier autonomous institution for wildlife research and management |
| **National Environmental Engineering Research Institute (NEERI)**| **Nagpur** (Maharashtra) | **1958**; pioneer in environmental monitoring, sewage, industrial pollution solutions (CSIR) |
| **Central Pollution Control Board (CPCB)** | **New Delhi** | **1974**; statutory organization under Water Act 1974; sets national air/water norms |
| **National Biodiversity Authority (NBA)** | **Chennai** (Tamil Nadu) | **2003**; statutory body under Biological Diversity Act 2002 |
| **Sálim Ali Centre for Ornithology & Natural History (SACON)** | **Coimbatore** (Tamil Nadu) | Dedicated to conservation of avifauna and wetland ecology |
| **Indian Institute of Forest Management (IIFM)**| **Bhopal** (Madhya Pradesh) | **1982**; premier forestry management and planning institute |
| **G.B. Pant National Institute of Himalayan Environment** | **Kosi-Katarmal, Almora (Uttarakhand)**| **1988**; apex research body on sustainable development and ecology of Indian Himalayas |
| **Central Arid Zone Research Institute (CAZRI)** | **Jodhpur** (Rajasthan) | **1959**; research on desertification and arid land agriculture |
| **National Disaster Management Authority (NDMA)**| **New Delhi** | **2005**; apex body under Disaster Management Act 2005; **Chaired by the Prime Minister** |

## 4. Indian Wildlife Conservation Projects (Chronological Ladder)

| Project Name | Year Launched | Launch Location / Nodal Agency | Key Objective / Status / Prelims Fact |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Project Tiger** | **1 April 1973** | **Jim Corbett National Park (Uttarakhand)** | Central scheme; initiated with 9 reserves under Kailash Sankhala ("Tiger Man of India"); statutory backing via NTCA (2006); **58 Tiger Reserves** (Madhav TR, MP, is the 58th). Corbett has among the highest tiger densities. |
| **Project Crocodile** | **1975** | Hyderabad / Odisha (with UNDP & FAO aid) | Conservation of 3 crocodilian species: Gharial (*Gavialis gangeticus*), Mugger crocodile, and Saltwater crocodile (Bhitarkanika, Odisha) |
| **Project Hangul** | **1970** | Dachigam National Park (J&K) | Conservation of Kashmir Stag (*Cervus elaphus hanglu* - critically endangered) with IUCN/WWF aid |
| **Project Rhino (IRV 2020)** | **1987 / 2005** | Assam (Kaziranga, Manas, Orang, Pobitora) | Indian Rhino Vision 2020 aimed to attain a wild population of at least 3,000 Greater One-Horned Rhinos in Assam |
| **Project Elephant** | **February 1992** | Jharkhand (Singhbhum) | Centrally sponsored; protects elephant habitats and corridors; addresses human-elephant conflict; **Mike sites** (Monitoring of Illegal Killing of Elephants); campaign: *Haathi Mere Saathi* |
| **Project Sea Turtle** | **1999** | Odisha coast (Gahirmatha / Rushikulya) | Conservation of **Olive Ridley turtles** (*Lepidochelys olivacea*) during synchronized mass nesting (**Arribada**) with MoEFCC & UNDP |
| **Project Snow Leopard** | **2009** | High-altitude Himalayan states (Hemis, Gangotri, Nanda Devi) | Conservation of high-altitude ecosystem and the elusive Ghost of the Mountains (*Panthera uncia*) |
| **Project Dolphin** | **2020** | Ministry of Environment, Forest & Climate Change | Gangetic Dolphin (*Platanista gangetica*) declared **National Aquatic Animal on 5 October 2009** (5 Oct celebrated as **National Dolphin Day**); blind; uses echolocation; Vikramshila Sanctuary (Bihar) |
| **Project Cheetah** | **September 2022** | **Kuno National Park (Madhya Pradesh)** | Intercontinental translocation of African cheetahs from Namibia (8) and South Africa (12); Asiatic Cheetah declared extinct in India in **1952** |

## 5. Ramsar Wetlands in Uttar Pradesh & Uttarakhand

- **Uttarakhand**:
  - **Asan Conservation Reserve** (Dehradun; confluence of Asan and Yamuna; **first Ramsar of Uttarakhand, Oct 2020**).
  - **Jhilmil Jheel Conservation Reserve** (Haridwar, **2005**) is the state’s swamp-deer / barasingha wetland — **not** the Ramsar first (that is Asan).
- **Uttar Pradesh (12 Ramsar sites)** — do not stop at the older list of 10:
  1. **Bakhira Wildlife Sanctuary** (Sant Kabir Nagar).
  2. **Haiderpur Wetland** (Muzaffarnagar–Bijnor; Solani / Hastinapur WLS).
  3. **Nawabganj Bird Sanctuary** (*Chandra Shekhar Azad*, Unnao).
  4. **Parvati Arga Bird Sanctuary** (Gonda).
  5. **Saman Bird Sanctuary** (Mainpuri).
  6. **Samaspur Bird Sanctuary** (Rae Bareli).
  7. **Sandi Bird Sanctuary** (Hardoi).
  8. **Sarsai Nawar Jheel** (Etawah; Sarus Crane).
  9. **Sur Sarovar (Keetham Lake)** (Agra).
  10. **Upper Ganga River** (Brijghat to Narora).
  11. **Patna Bird Sanctuary** (Etah).
  12. **Shekha Jheel** (Aligarh).

> **Traps:** Sultanpur NP = **Haryana**. Rudrasagar = **Tripura**. Samaspur and Sarsai Nawar **are** in UP.

### 5A. Protected areas of UP & UK — Declared / still in force

| PA | State | From (year) | High-yield |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Hailey → Ramganga → Jim Corbett NP / TR** | **Uttarakhand** | NP **1936** (Hailey); Project Tiger launch site **1973** | India’s first National Park. Nainital–Pauri belt. |
| **Rajaji NP / TR** | **Uttarakhand** | NP **1983**; tiger reserve **2015** | Elephant corridor; Haridwar–Dehradun–Pauri |
| **Nanda Devi NP** | **Uttarakhand** | **1982** (BR **1988**; UNESCO mixed serial with VoF) | Chamoli; Nanda Devi peak |
| **Valley of Flowers NP** | **Uttarakhand** | **1982** (UNESCO **1988**, expanded **2005**) | Smallest UK NP; Chamoli |
| **Gangotri NP** | **Uttarakhand** | **1989** | Largest UK NP (~2390 sq km); Uttarkashi |
| **Govind NP / WLS** | **Uttarakhand** | WLS **1955**; NP later | Snow leopard belt; Uttarkashi |
| **Dudhwa NP / TR** | **Uttar Pradesh** | NP **1977**; TR **1987** | Lakhimpur Kheri Terai; rhino from **1984**; barasingha |
| **Pilibhit TR** | **Uttar Pradesh** | **2014** | Terai; tiger + woodland swamp deer |
| **Ranipur TR** | **Uttar Pradesh** | **2022/2023** | Chitrakoot–Banda (Vindhyan); not Terai |
| **Hastinapur WLS** | **Uttar Pradesh** | **1986** | **Largest UP sanctuary**; Meerut–Bijnor belt; Haiderpur Ramsar inside |
| **Katarniaghat WLS** | **Uttar Pradesh** | **1975** | Terai Arc toward Nepal; gharial / dolphin |

UP has **no** biosphere reserve and **no** marine PA. UK biosphere = **Nanda Devi (1988; UNESCO MAB 2004)**.

## 6. National Air Quality Index (AQI)

Launched in **October 2014** under Swachh Bharat Abhiyan by CPCB:
- **Six Categories**:
  1. *Good* (0–50)
  2. *Satisfactory* (51–100)
  3. *Moderately Polluted* (101–200)
  4. *Poor* (201–300)
  5. *Very Poor* (301–400)
  6. *Severe* (401–500).
- **Eight Monitored Pollutants (Frequently Tested in PCS!)**:
  1. **Particulate Matter 10 (PM10)**
  2. **Particulate Matter 2.5 (PM2.5)**
  3. **Nitrogen Dioxide (NO2)**
  4. **Sulphur Dioxide (SO2)**
  5. **Carbon Monoxide (CO)**
  6. **Ozone (O3)**
  7. **Ammonia (NH3)**
  8. **Lead (Pb)**.
  *(Notice: Carbon Dioxide (CO2) is NOT part of the AQI 8 pollutants!)*

## 8. International Environmental Conventions & Protocols

| Year | Convention / Agreement | Place / City | Primary Objective / Regulatory Scope |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1971 (2 Feb)** | **Ramsar Convention on Wetlands** | Ramsar (Iran) | Conservation and wise use of wetlands; entered into force 1975; **World Wetlands Day (2 Feb)**; India joined **1 Feb 1982**; Keoladeo & Chilika were first sites; **Montreux Record** (Keoladeo NP & Loktak Lake) |
| **1972 (5–16 Jun)** | **Stockholm Conference (UNCHE)** | Stockholm (Sweden) | UN Conference on the Human Environment; **Created UNEP (Nairobi, Kenya)**; celebrated as **World Environment Day (5 June)** |
| **1973 (3 Mar)** | **CITES (Washington Convention)** | Washington, D.C. (USA) | Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora; Appendix I, II, III; **World Wildlife Day (3 March)** |
| **1979** | **Bonn Convention (CMS)** | Bonn (Germany) | Convention on the Conservation of Migratory Species of Wild Animals |
| **1982** | **UNCLOS** | Montego Bay (Jamaica) | Law of the Sea: Territorial waters (12 nm), Contiguous zone (24 nm), Exclusive Economic Zone (200 nm) |
| **1985** | **Vienna Convention** | Vienna (Austria) | Framework convention for the protection of the stratospheric ozone layer |
| **1987 (16 Sep)** | **Montreal Protocol** | Montreal (Canada) | **Legally binding phase-out of Ozone Depleting Substances (ODS / CFCs)**; entered into force 1 Jan 1989; **World Ozone Day (16 Sept)**; **Kigali Amendment (2016)** phase out HFCs |
| **1987** | **Brundtland Report** | UN World Commission | Report titled ***Our Common Future***; formally defined **Sustainable Development** |
| **1989** | **Basel Convention** | Basel (Switzerland) | Control of transboundary movements of **Hazardous Wastes and their disposal**; entered into force 1992 |
| **1992 (3–14 Jun)** | **Rio Earth Summit (UNCED)** | Rio de Janeiro (Brazil) | Landmark summit producing 3 Rio Conventions:  <br>1. **UNFCCC** (Climate Change)  <br>2. **CBD** (Biological Diversity)  <br>3. **UNCCD** (Combating Desertification)  <br>Also produced: **Agenda 21**, Rio Declaration, and Forest Principles |
| **1997 (11 Dec)** | **Kyoto Protocol** | Kyoto (Japan) | UNFCCC COP-3; **Legally binding greenhouse gas emission reduction targets for Annex-I developed countries**; 6 greenhouse gases (CO2, CH4, N2O, HFCs, PFCs, SF6); Clean Development Mechanism (CDM); entered into force 2005; expired 2020 |
| **1998** | **Rotterdam Convention** | Rotterdam (Netherlands) | Prior Informed Consent (PIC) procedure for certain hazardous chemicals and pesticides in international trade |
| **2000** | **Cartagena Protocol on Biosafety** | Montreal / Cartagena | Protocol to CBD governing the safe transfer, handling, and use of **Living Modified Organisms (LMOs)** |
| **2001** | **Stockholm Convention on POPs** | Stockholm (Sweden) | Elimination or restriction of **Persistent Organic Pollutants (POPs)** (original "Dirty Dozen" including DDT, Dioxins, PCBs) |
| **2010** | **Nagoya Protocol** | Nagoya (Japan) | Supplementary agreement to CBD on **Access to Genetic Resources and the Fair and Equitable Sharing of Benefits (ABS)** |
| **2013** | **Minamata Convention** | Kumamoto (Japan) | Global treaty to protect human health and the environment from adverse effects of **Mercury (Hg)** |
| **2015 (12 Dec)** | **Paris Agreement** | Paris (France) | UNFCCC COP-21; keep global temperature rise **well below 2°C** above pre-industrial levels, pursue efforts to limit rise to **1.5°C**; Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs); entered into force **4 Nov 2016** |
| **2015** | **Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs)** | UN General Assembly | **17 Goals, 169 Targets (2015–2030)**; SDG 1: No Poverty, SDG 2: Zero Hunger, SDG 3: Good Health, SDG 4: Quality Education, SDG 5: Gender Equality, SDG 6: Clean Water & Sanitation, SDG 13: Climate Action, SDG 14: Life Below Water, SDG 15: Life on Land |
| **2022** | **Kunming-Montreal GBF** | Montreal (CBD COP-15) | Global Biodiversity Framework; includes landmark **"30x30 target"** (protect at least 30% of planet's land and ocean by 2030) |

## 9. Fundamental Ecological Principles & Laws

### A. Lindeman’s 10% Energy Law (1942)
- Formulated by **Raymond Lindeman**.
- Only **approximately 10% of the energy** entering any trophic level is transferred and organized into organic matter at the next higher trophic level.
- 90% of energy is lost as metabolic heat, respiration, and catabolism.
- Explains why food chains rarely exceed 4 to 5 trophic levels.

### B. Ecological Pyramids (Charles Elton, 1927)
1. **Pyramid of Energy**:
   - **ALWAYS UPRIGHT, WITHOUT EXCEPTION!**
   - Energy decreases progressively at each successive trophic level according to thermodynamics.
2. **Pyramid of Biomass**:
   - **Terrestrial Ecosystems (Grassland, Forest)**: **UPRIGHT** (Producer biomass > Herbivore > Carnivore).
   - **Aquatic Ecosystems (Pond, Lake, Ocean)**: **INVERTED** (Phytoplankton standing crop biomass is much smaller than zooplankton and predatory fish).
3. **Pyramid of Numbers**:
   - **Grassland & Cropland**: **UPRIGHT** (Millions of grasses support fewer herbivores, and even fewer carnivores).
   - **Tree / Parasitic Ecosystem**: **INVERTED** (A single large tree supports hundreds of birds, which host thousands of parasites and hyper-parasites).
   - **Forest Ecosystem**: **Spindle-shaped** (Few large trees support many herbivores, which are eaten by fewer carnivores).

### C. Bioaccumulation vs Biomagnification
- **Bioaccumulation**: Increase in the concentration of a chemical pollutant in an **individual organism over time** (rate of intake > rate of excretion).
- **Biomagnification**: Progressive increase in the concentration of persistent, non-biodegradable, fat-soluble toxins (e.g., **DDT, Mercury, PCBs**) at **successively higher trophic levels** of a food chain.
  - *Classic example:* Phytoplankton (0.00005 ppm) → Zooplankton → Small Fish → Fish-eating birds / Falcons (25 ppm causing egg-shell thinning and population collapse).

### D. Greenhouse Gases: Global Warming Potential (GWP, 100-Year Horizon)
*Ranked from highest warming impact per molecule to lowest (relative to CO2 = 1):*

**SF6 (23,500) > PFCs (7,000–9,000) > HFCs (1,400–3,000) > N2O (273) > CH4 (28) > CO2 (1)**

- **Water Vapour**: Most abundant natural greenhouse gas contributing greatest share to natural greenhouse effect (~60%), but has short atmospheric residence time.
- **Carbon Dioxide (CO2)**: Primary anthropogenic driver of global climate change due to massive volume and century-long residence time.
- **Methane (CH4)**: Major sources: Paddy/rice fields, ruminant livestock enteric fermentation, landfills, wetlands, coal mining.
