<div id="subject-08-indian-economy" class="subject-banner">
<span class="badge">Subject 08 · Economy</span>
<h1>SUBJECT 08: INDIAN ECONOMY</h1>
<h2>भारतीय अर्थव्यवस्था — दैनिक उच्च-प्राथमिकता तथ्य</h2>
<p>National Income Aggregates, 12 Five-Year Plans & Models, RBI Monetary Policy (Repo/SDF/CRR), CPI vs WPI, Fiscal Deficits, Finance Commission (15th/16th), and Poverty Committees.</p>
</div>

## 1. National Income Aggregates & Macroeconomic Formulas

| Concept / Metric | Formula / Technical Definition | Key Exam Traps & Prelims Triggers |
|---|---|---|
| **GDP at Market Price** | $GDP_{MP} = C + I + G + (X - M)$ | Total market value of all final goods & services produced **within the domestic territory** in 1 year. Foreigners earning in India included; Indians earning abroad excluded. |
| **GNP at Market Price** | $GNP_{MP} = GDP_{MP} + NFIA$ | Measures income earned by **nationals/citizens** regardless of geographical location. $NFIA$ = Net Factor Income from Abroad (Factor income from abroad $-$ Factor income paid abroad). |
| **NNP at Market Price** | $NNP_{MP} = GNP_{MP} - \text{Depreciation}$ | Deducts consumption of fixed capital (depreciation). **Depreciation is also called Capital Consumption Allowance**. |
| **NNP at Factor Cost (National Income)** | $NNP_{FC} = NNP_{MP} - \text{Net Indirect Taxes (NIT)}$ | Traditional official definition of **National Income**. Net Indirect Taxes (NIT) = Indirect Taxes $-$ Subsidies. |
| **Gross Value Added (GVA)** | $GVA_{Basic} = GVA_{Factor\ Cost} + \text{Production Taxes} - \text{Production Subsidies}$ | Since 2015, India measures headline GDP as: **$GDP_{MP} = GVA_{Basic} + \text{Product Taxes} - \text{Product Subsidies}$**. Base year changed to **2011–12**. |
| **Personal Disposable Income (PDI)** | $PDI = \text{Personal Income} - \text{Direct Taxes} - \text{Miscellaneous Fees/Fines}$ | Actual income available to households for consumption expenditure and savings. |
| **Real GDP vs Nominal GDP** | $\text{Real GDP} = \frac{\text{Nominal GDP}}{\text{GDP Deflator}} \times 100$ | Real GDP is calculated at **constant base year prices** (eliminates inflation impact). Nominal GDP is at current prices. |
| **GDP Deflator** | $\frac{\text{Nominal GDP}}{\text{Real GDP}} \times 100$ | Most comprehensive measure of inflation in an economy because it covers **all goods and services** produced, unlike CPI/WPI which use a fixed basket. |

> **Examiner Trap:** Transfer payments (old age pensions, unemployment allowance, student scholarships) are **excluded** from National Income because no corresponding production of goods or services occurs. Second-hand goods sales are also excluded (only broker commission is included).

---

## 2. Five-Year Plans (FYP) & NITI Aayog Master Matrix

| Plan & Duration | Model / Architect | Core Objective / Strategy | Key Milestones & Exam Triggers |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1st FYP (1951–56)** | **Harrod-Domar Model** | Agriculture, irrigation, price stability | Bhakra-Nangal, Hirakud, Damodar Valley dams launched; Target 2.1%, Achieved 3.6%. |
| **2nd FYP (1956–61)** | **P.C. Mahalanobis Model** | Rapid industrialisation, Heavy & Basic Industries | Steel plants at **Bhilai** (USSR), **Rourkela** (Germany), **Durgapur** (UK); Industrial Policy Resolution 1956. |
| **3rd FYP (1961–66)** | John Sandy & Sukhamoy Chakravarty | Self-reliant & self-generating economy | **Bokaro Steel Plant** (1964, USSR), FCI & CACP set up (1965). Plan failed due to 1962 China War, 1965 Pak War, 1965–66 Severe Drought. |
| **Plan Holiday (1966–69)** | Three Annual Plans | Green Revolution rollout | Devaluation of Indian Rupee (1966); High-Yielding Varieties (HYV) seeds introduced. |
| **4th FYP (1969–74)** | D.R. Gadgil Formula | Growth with stability & progressive self-reliance | **14 Major Commercial Banks Nationalised** (19 July 1969); Pokhran-I Nuclear Test (*Smiling Buddha*, 1974); 1971 Indo-Pak War. |
| **5th FYP (1974–79)** | D.P. Dhar Model | **Garibi Hatao** (Poverty Eradication) & Self-reliance | **20-Point Programme** (1975); Minimum Needs Programme (MNP); Terminated 1 year early in 1978 by Janata Govt. |
| **Rolling Plan (1978–80)** | Gunnar Myrdal concept | Annual revisions without fixed 5-year lock | Introduced by Janata Govt (Morarji Desai); ended when Congress returned in 1980. |
| **6th FYP (1980–85)** | Investment & Modernisation | Poverty eradication, economic & tech self-reliance | **NABARD established** (12 July 1982, Shivaraman Committee); **EXIM Bank** (1982); **6 more banks nationalised** (1980). |
| **7th FYP (1985–90)** | Pranab Mukherjee / Lakdawala | Food, Work & Productivity | Speed Post (1986); SEBI formed (1988); Jawahar Rozgar Yojana (JRY, 1989). |
| **Annual Plans (1990–92)** | Balance of Payments Crisis | Structural reforms & crisis stabilization | **LPG Reforms (1991)**: Liberalisation, Privatisation, Globalisation under P.V. Narasimha Rao & Dr. Manmohan Singh. |
| **8th FYP (1992–97)** | John W. Miller Model | Human Resource Development (employment, education) | India joined **WTO** (1 Jan 1995); National Stock Exchange (NSE) operationalised. |
| **9th FYP (1997–2002)** | Social Justice & Equality | Growth with Social Justice and Equity | 50 years of Indian Independence; Asian Financial Crisis backdrop. |
| **10th FYP (2002–07)** | Target 8% GDP Growth | Doubling per capita income in 10 years, reduce poverty by 5% | National Rural Employment Guarantee Act (**NREGA**, 2005); Bharat Nirman. |
| **11th FYP (2007–12)** | C. Rangarajan / Montek Singh | **Faster and More Inclusive Growth** | Right to Education (RTE Act, 2009); National Rural Livelihoods Mission (NRLM). |
| **12th FYP (2012–17)** | Final Five-Year Plan | **Faster, More Inclusive and Sustainable Growth** | Terminated; replaced by NITI Aayog 15-Year Vision, 7-Year Strategy & 3-Year Action Agenda. |

> **NITI Aayog (National Institution for Transforming India):** Formed on **1 January 2015** replacing the Planning Commission (15 March 1950). Non-constitutional, non-statutory extra-constitutional think tank. Chairperson: **Prime Minister**. Governing Council comprises all State Chief Ministers, UT Lt Governors/CMs. Operates on **Bottom-Up** approach and **Cooperative Federalism** (unlike top-down Planning Commission).

---

## 3. Monetary Policy, RBI Instruments & Money Supply Aggregates

### A. RBI Monetary Policy Instruments Master Table
| Instrument | Nature / Category | Operational Mechanism & Definition |
|---|---|---|
| **Cash Reserve Ratio (CRR)** | Quantitative / Direct | Percentage of Net Demand and Time Liabilities (NDTL) commercial banks **must keep with RBI in cash**. No interest paid by RBI on CRR. Governed by RBI Act, 1934 (Sec 42). |
| **Statutory Liquidity Ratio (SLR)** | Quantitative / Direct | Percentage of NDTL commercial banks **must maintain with themselves** in approved liquid assets (Cash, Gold, Govt Securities). Governed by Banking Regulation Act, 1949 (Sec 24). |
| **Repo Rate** | Quantitative / Indirect | Rate at which RBI lends short-term money to commercial banks against **government securities as collateral** (repurchase agreement). Primary policy rate. |
| **Reverse Repo Rate** | Quantitative / Indirect | Rate at which RBI borrows short-term funds from commercial banks against government securities. |
| **Standing Deposit Facility (SDF)** | Quantitative / Liquidity | Introduced in April 2022 under amended RBI Act (Sec 17). Allows RBI to absorb excess liquidity from commercial banks **without providing any collateral (G-Secs)**. Replaced Reverse Repo as floor of LAF corridor. |
| **Marginal Standing Facility (MSF)** | Quantitative / Liquidity | Penal rate at which scheduled commercial banks can borrow overnight funds from RBI by dipping into their SLR quota (up to prescribed limit). Ceiling of LAF corridor. |
| **Bank Rate** | Quantitative / Long-term | Rate at which RBI buys or rediscounts bills of exchange or commercial papers; benchmark for penal interest rates on non-compliance with reserve requirements. |
| **Open Market Operations (OMO)** | Quantitative / Market | Outright purchase and sale of Government Securities (G-Secs) by RBI in open market to inject or absorb durable liquidity. |
| **Moral Suasion, Credit Rationing, Margin Requirements** | Qualitative / Selective | Directives to banks to channel credit to specific priority sectors, control speculative borrowing, and regulate loan-to-value (LTV) ratios. |

### B. Money Supply Measures (Monetary Aggregates)
- **Reserve Money ($M_0$ / High Powered Money):** Currency in Circulation $+$ Bankers' deposits with RBI $+$ 'Other' deposits with RBI.
- **Narrow Money ($M_1$):** Currency with Public $+$ Demand Deposits with Banking System (Current & Savings Account - CASA) $+$ 'Other' deposits with RBI. *(Most liquid aggregate).*
- **$M_2$:** $M_1 +$ Post Office Savings Bank deposits.
- **Broad Money ($M_3$):** $M_1 +$ Time Deposits (Fixed/Recurring Deposits) with the banking system. *(Most commonly used indicator of money supply in India).*
- **$M_4$:** $M_3 +$ All deposits with Post Office Savings Organisation (excluding National Savings Certificates / NSC).
- **Liquidity Hierarchy:** $M_1 > M_2 > M_3 > M_4$.

### C. Monetary Policy Committee (MPC)
- Statutory body constituted under **Section 45ZB of RBI Act, 1934** (amended by Finance Act, 2016).
- **6 Members:** 3 from RBI (RBI Governor as ex-officio Chairperson, Deputy Governor in charge of monetary policy, 1 RBI officer) $+$ 3 external members appointed by Central Government.
- **Inflation Target:** Central Govt in consultation with RBI sets retail inflation target (CPI headline) at **4% with a tolerance band of $\pm 2\%$** (i.e., 2% to 6%).
- Meets at least **4 times a year**. Quorum is 4 members. Each member has 1 vote; RBI Governor has a **casting vote** in case of a tie.

---

## 4. Inflation Indices: CPI vs WPI Master Comparison

| Parameter | Wholesale Price Index (WPI) | Consumer Price Index (CPI - Combined) |
|---|---|---|
| **Publishing Authority** | Office of Economic Adviser (DPIIT), Ministry of Commerce & Industry | National Statistical Office (NSO), Ministry of Statistics and Programme Implementation (MoSPI) |
| **Base Year** | **2011–12** (Revised from 2004–05 in 2017) | **2012** |
| **Official Headline Anchor** | Used for wholesale trade transactions | **Official headline anchor for RBI Monetary Policy** (since Urjit Patel Committee, 2014) |
| **Services Included?** | **NO services included** (Goods only) | **YES, Services are included** (Housing, Transport, Education, Medical, etc.) |
| **Number of Commodities** | **697 items** | 448 items (Rural), 460 items (Urban) |
| **Highest Weight Group** | **Manufactured Products (64.23%)** > Primary Articles (22.62%) > Fuel & Power (13.15%) | **Food and Beverages (45.86%)** > Misc/Services (28.32%) > Housing (10.07%) |
| **Price Point Measured** | First point of bulk commercial transaction | Retail consumer purchase point |

---

## 5. Public Finance, Deficits & Finance Commission

### A. Deficit Formulas Master Sheet
1. **Fiscal Deficit (FD):** $\text{Total Expenditure} - \text{Total Receipts (excluding borrowings)}$. It represents the total borrowing requirements of the government from all sources in that year.
2. **Revenue Deficit (RD):** $\text{Revenue Expenditure} - \text{Revenue Receipts}$. Indicates that the government is borrowing to finance day-to-day consumption rather than capital asset creation.
3. **Effective Revenue Deficit (ERD):** $\text{Revenue Deficit} - \text{Grants for creation of capital assets}$. Introduced in Union Budget 2011–12.
4. **Primary Deficit (PD):** $\text{Fiscal Deficit} - \text{Interest Payments}$. Measures the current year's fiscal stance excluding the burden of past accumulated debt interest.

### B. Finance Commission (Article 280) Master Matrix
- Quasi-judicial body constituted by the **President every 5 years** under Article 280.
- Recommends devolution of net proceeds of taxes between Union and States (Vertical devolution) and allocation among States (Horizontal devolution).
- **1st FC:** K.C. Neogy (1951)
- **13th FC:** Dr. Vijay Kelkar (recommending GST framework)
- **14th FC:** Dr. Y.V. Reddy (increased States' share sharply from 32% to **42%**)
- **15th FC:** **N.K. Singh** (2020–26): Devolution share set at **41%** (1% adjusted for newly created UTs of J&K and Ladakh). Criteria weights: Income Distance (45%), Population 2011 (15%), Area (15%), Forest & Ecology (10%), Demographic Performance (12.5%), Tax Effort (2.5%).
- **16th FC:** Constituted on 31 Dec 2023 under chairmanship of **Dr. Arvind Panagariya** (former Vice Chairman of NITI Aayog).

---

## 6. Banking & Financial Sector Milestones

- **1935 (1 April):** RBI established on recommendations of **Hilton Young Commission (1926)** under RBI Act, 1934. Initial capital ₹5 crore; nationalised on **1 January 1949**.
- **1955:** Imperial Bank of India (formed in 1921 by amalgamating Presidency Banks of Bengal, Bombay, Madras) nationalised and renamed **State Bank of India (SBI)** on recommendation of All India Rural Credit Survey Committee (AD Gorwala).
- **1969 (19 July):** **14 Major Commercial Banks** (with deposits $\ge ₹50$ crore) nationalised by Indira Gandhi Govt via Banking Companies (Acquisition and Transfer of Undertakings) Ordinance.
- **1975 (2 October):** First Regional Rural Bank (**Prathama Bank**, Moradabad, UP) established on recommendations of **M. Narasimham Working Group**.
- **1980 (15 April):** **6 More Commercial Banks** (with deposits $\ge ₹200$ crore) nationalised.
- **1982 (12 July):** **NABARD** established under NABARD Act, 1981 on recommendation of **B. Sivaraman Committee**.
- **1988:** SEBI set up as non-statutory body; granted statutory powers via **SEBI Act, 1992** on 30 January 1992.
- **Priority Sector Lending (PSL) Targets:** Total PSL target for Domestic Scheduled Commercial Banks and Foreign Banks with $\ge 20$ branches is **40% of Adjusted Net Bank Credit (ANBC)**. Sub-targets: Agriculture (18%, within which 10% for Small & Marginal Farmers), Micro Enterprises (7.5%), Weaker Sections (12%). For Regional Rural Banks (RRBs) and Small Finance Banks (SFBs), total PSL target is **75% of ANBC**.

---

## 7. Agricultural Revolutions, Schemes & MSP

### A. Colour Revolutions in Indian Agriculture
| Colour Revolution | Associated Sector / Commodity | Pioneer / Father in India |
|---|---|---|
| **Green Revolution** | Food Grains (Wheat & Rice, HYV seeds) | **M.S. Swaminathan** (Norman Borlaug globally) |
| **White Revolution (Operation Flood)** | Milk & Dairy Development (1970) | **Dr. Verghese Kurien** (Amul / NDDB Anand) |
| **Blue Revolution** | Fisheries & Aquaculture | Dr. Hiralal Chaudhuri & Dr. Arun Krishnan |
| **Yellow Revolution** | Oilseeds (Mustard, Sunflower, Soyabean) | Sam Pitroda |
| **Golden Revolution** | Horticulture, Honey, Fruits | Nirpakh Tutej |
| **Golden Fibre Revolution** | **Jute** production | - |
| **Silver Revolution** | Eggs & Poultry | Indira Gandhi |
| **Silver Fibre Revolution** | **Cotton** production | - |
| **Pink Revolution** | Onion, Pharmaceuticals, Prawn | Durgesh Patel |
| **Red Revolution** | Meat & Tomato production | Vishal Tewari |
| **Round Revolution** | **Potato** production | - |
| **Grey Revolution** | Fertilizers | - |
| **Brown Revolution** | Leather, Non-conventional energy, Cocoa | - |
| **Rainbow Revolution** | Integrated development of all agri sectors | Announced in Agri Policy 2000 |

### B. Minimum Support Price (MSP) Mechanism
- Announced by Central Government on recommendations of **CACP (Commission for Agricultural Costs and Prices)** before sowing season.
- Covers **22 mandated crops** $+$ Fair and Remunerative Price (FRP) for **Sugarcane**:
  - 7 Cereals (Paddy, Wheat, Maize, Jowar, Bajra, Ragi, Barley)
  - 5 Pulses (Gram, Arhar/Tur, Moong, Urad, Masur)
  - 8 Oilseeds (Groundnut, Rapeseed/Mustard, Soyabean, Sesamum, Sunflower, Safflower, Nigerseed, Copra)
  - Raw Cotton, Raw Jute, De-husked Coconut
- **Cost Concepts used by CACP:**
  - $A2$: Direct paid-out costs on seeds, fertilizers, pesticides, hired labour, fuel, irrigation.
  - $A2 + FL$: $A2 +$ Imputed value of unpaid family labour.
  - $C2$: Comprehensive cost ($A2 + FL +$ rental value of owned land $+$ interest on owned capital assets).
  - Central Govt formula since Union Budget 2018–19: MSP set at **at least 1.5 times ($150\%$) of $A2 + FL$**.

---

## 8. Poverty Estimation Committees & Multidimensional Poverty

| Committee & Year | Consumption Basket / Criteria Used | Poverty Line Benchmark (Monthly / Daily) |
|---|---|---|
| **Y.K. Alagh (1979)** | Minimum Nutritional Calorie intake | **2,400 kcal/day** in Rural areas; **2,100 kcal/day** in Urban areas. |
| **D.T. Lakdawala (1993)** | Calorie norms linked with state-specific price indices (CPI-AL for rural, CPI-IW for urban) | State-specific poverty lines; rejected uniform national basket. |
| **Suresh Tendulkar (2009)** | Shifted from calories to mixed reference basket including **Health, Education, Clothing, Footwear** | Set per capita daily consumption at **₹27.2 in rural** and **₹33.3 in urban** areas (₹816/month rural, ₹1000/month urban at 2011–12 prices). National poverty rate: **21.9%** (2011–12). |
| **C. Rangarajan (2014)** | Independent nutritional norm (2,155 kcal rural, 2,090 kcal urban + protein/fat) + non-food expenses | Set per capita daily consumption at **₹32 in rural** and **₹47 in urban** areas (₹972/month rural, ₹1407/month urban). National poverty rate: **29.5%** (2011–12). |

> **Multidimensional Poverty Index (MPI) by NITI Aayog:**  
> Follows Alkire-Foster methodology. **3 Dimensions and 12 Indicators** (each dimension weighted equally at $1/3$):
> 1. **Health (1/3):** Nutrition (1/6), Child & Adolescent Mortality (1/12), Maternal Health (1/12).
> 2. **Education (1/3):** Years of Schooling (1/6), School Attendance (1/6).
> 3. **Standard of Living (1/3):** Cooking Fuel, Sanitation, Drinking Water, Electricity, Housing, Assets, Bank Account (each 1/21).

---

## 9. International Financial Institutions & Global Economic Reports

| Institution / Group | Established | Headquarters | Key Publications / Reports |
|---|---|---|---|
| **International Monetary Fund (IMF)** | 1944 (Bretton Woods) | **Washington D.C.** | **World Economic Outlook (WEO)**, **Global Financial Stability Report (GFSR)**, Fiscal Monitor |
| **World Bank (IBRD + IDA)** | 1944 (Bretton Woods) | **Washington D.C.** | **Global Economic Prospects (GEP)**, World Development Report (WDR) |
| **World Economic Forum (WEF)** | 1971 (Klaus Schwab) | **Cologny/Geneva (Switzerland)** | **Global Competitiveness Report**, **Global Gender Gap Report**, Global Risks Report, Energy Transition Index |
| **World Trade Organisation (WTO)** | 1 Jan 1995 (Marrakesh) | **Geneva (Switzerland)** | World Trade Report |
| **Asian Development Bank (ADB)** | 1966 | **Mandaluyong, Manila (Philippines)** | Asian Development Outlook (ADO) |
| **Asian Infrastructure Investment Bank (AIIB)** | 2016 | **Beijing (China)** | China largest shareholder, India 2nd largest shareholder |
| **New Development Bank (NDB / BRICS Bank)** | 2014 (Fortaleza) | **Shanghai (China)** | First President: K.V. Kamath (India) |
| **UNDP** | 1965 | **New York (USA)** | **Human Development Report (HDR)** — HDI, IHDI, GII, MPI |

> **Special Drawing Rights (SDR - Paper Gold):** International reserve asset created by the IMF in 1969. The SDR basket includes **5 currencies**: US Dollar, Euro, Chinese Renminbi (Yuan - added in 2016), Japanese Yen, and British Pound Sterling.

---

<h1>SUBJECT 09: CENSUS & URBANISATION (INDIA & UP)</h1>
<h2>जनगणना एवं नगरीकरण — दैनिक उच्च-प्राथमिकता तथ्य</h2>
<p>Census 2011 National Indicators, State Top & Bottom Extremes, SC & ST Demographics, Religious Hierarchy, Uttar Pradesh District Extremes, and Urbanisation Rates in India & UP.</p>
</div>

## 1. Indian Census History & Legal Framework

| Milestone / Parameter | Year / Detail | Historical / Legal Context |
|---|---|---|
| **First Census in Modern India** | **1872** | Conducted non-synchronously under **Lord Mayo**. |
| **First Synchronous Decennial Census** | **1881** | Conducted across all India under Viceroy **Lord Ripon** (Census Commissioner: **W.C. Plowden**). |
| **The Year of the Great Divide** | **1921** | The only census decade in Indian history showing a **negative population growth rate (-0.31%)** due to 1918 Spanish Flu pandemic, plague, and severe famine. |
| **First Census of Independent India** | **1951** | Census Commissioner: **R.A. Gopalaswami**. Total population: 361 million; sex ratio: 946. |
| **Census Act, 1948** | Enacted under Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel | Permanent statutory basis; census figures are strictly confidential and inadmissible in courts as evidence. |
| **Nodal Authority** | Registrar General and Census Commissioner of India (RGCCI) | Under **Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA)**, New Delhi. |
| **2011 Census Edition** | **15th Census of India** (7th after Independence) | Motto: *"Our Census, Our Future"*; Mascot: Woman Enumerator; Commissioner: **Dr. C. Chandramouli**. |

---

## 2. Census 2011 Master Matrix: National Indicators

| Demographic Indicator | Census 2001 | Census 2011 | Absolute / Percentage Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Total Population** | 1,028.7 million | **1,210.85 million** (~121.08 Cr) | $+182.1$ million (+17.7%) |
| **Male Population** | 532.2 million (51.74%) | **623.27 million (51.54%)** | Male proportion decreased slightly |
| **Female Population** | 496.5 million (48.26%) | **587.58 million (48.46%)** | Female proportion increased |
| **Decadal Growth Rate (2001–11)** | 21.54% | **17.70%** | Decreased by **3.84 percentage points** |
| **Annual Exponential Growth Rate** | 1.97% | **1.64%** | Decelerating trend |
| **Population Density (persons/sq km)** | 325 | **382** | Increased by **+57 persons/sq km** |
| **Overall Sex Ratio (females/1000 males)** | 933 | **943** | Improved by **+10 points** |
| **Child Sex Ratio (0–6 years)** | 927 | **918** | **Declined by -9 points** (Critical alarm zone) |
| **Child Population (0–6 years) share** | 15.93% | **13.59%** | Reflects falling fertility rates |
| **Overall Literacy Rate** | 64.83% | **73.00%** (some official reports 74.04%) | Increased by **+8.17 percentage points** |
| **Male Literacy Rate** | 75.26% | **80.90%** (or 82.14%) | Improved |
| **Female Literacy Rate** | 53.67% | **64.60%** (or 65.46%) | Faster gain than males (+10.93%) |
| **Gender Gap in Literacy** | 21.59% | **16.30%** (or 16.68%) | Narrowed by **5.29 percentage points** |
| **Urban Population Share** | 27.81% | **31.16%** | Increased by $+3.35$ percentage points |
| **Rural Population Share** | 72.19% | **68.84%** | Decreased proportionally |

## 4. Scheduled Castes (SC) and Scheduled Tribes (ST) Demographics

### A. Scheduled Castes (SC) — Census 2011
- Total SC Population in India: **201.4 million** (**16.6%** of total population).
- **Highest SC Population (Absolute):** **Uttar Pradesh** (41.35 million, accounts for 20.5% of total Indian SCs) > West Bengal (21.46M) > Bihar (16.57M).
- **Highest SC Percentage (% of State Population):** **Punjab** (**31.9%**) > Himachal Pradesh (25.2%) > West Bengal (23.5%) > Uttar Pradesh (20.7%).
- **States/UTs with NO Scheduled Castes:** **Nagaland, Mizoram, Lakshadweep, Andaman & Nicobar Islands**.

### B. Scheduled Tribes (ST) — Census 2011
- Total ST Population in India: **104.3 million** (**8.6%** of total population).
- **Highest ST Population (Absolute):** **Madhya Pradesh** (15.31 million, 14.7% of all Indian STs) > Maharashtra (10.51M) > Odisha (9.59M).
- **Highest ST Percentage (% of State/UT):** **Lakshadweep** (**94.8%**) and **Mizoram** (**94.4%**) > Nagaland (86.5%) > Meghalaya (86.1%).
- **States/UTs with NO Scheduled Tribes:** **Punjab, Haryana, Chandigarh, Delhi, Puducherry**.

## 7. Uttarakhand Census 2011 Highlights

- **Total Population:** **10,086,292** (~1.01 Crore, 0.83% of India).
- **Decadal Growth Rate:** **18.81%** (Plains growing rapidly: US Nagar 33.4%, Haridwar 30.6%; Hill districts showing negative growth: **Almora -1.28%**, **Pauri Garhwal -1.41%** - ghost villages phenomenon).
- **Population Density:** **189 persons/sq km** (Haridwar highest at 801; Uttarkashi lowest at 41).
- **Overall Sex Ratio:** **963 females/1000 males** (Almora highest at 1,139; Haridwar lowest at 880).
- **Child Sex Ratio (0–6 years):** **890** (Pithoragarh lowest at 816).
- **Overall Literacy Rate:** **78.82%** (Male 87.40%, Female 70.01%). Highest literacy: **Dehradun (84.25%)**; Lowest: **Haridwar (73.43%)**.

<div id="subject-10-uttar-pradesh-special-gk" class="subject-banner">
<span class="badge">Subject 10 · UP Special GK</span>
<h1>SUBJECT 10: UTTAR PRADESH SPECIAL GK</h1>
<h2>उत्तर प्रदेश विशेष सामान्य ज्ञान — दैनिक उच्च-प्राथमिकता तथ्य</h2>
<p>Geographic Boundaries & 4 Extremes, Drainage & Rivers, Dudhwa NP & 4 Tiger Reserves, 10 Ramsar Sites, 9 Agro-Climatic Zones, Minerals, 1857 Revolt, Gharanas, and 75 Districts ODOP.</p>
</div>

## 1. Uttar Pradesh Geographical Profile & Boundary Matrix

| Geographical Dimension | Exact Specification & Measurement | High-Yield Prelims Anchor |
|---|---|---|
| **Latitudinal Extent** | $23^{\circ} 52'\text{ N}$ to $30^{\circ} 24'\text{ N}$ | Latitudinal span: $6^{\circ} 32'$ |
| **Longitudinal Extent** | $77^{\circ} 05'\text{ E}$ to $84^{\circ} 38'\text{ E}$ | Longitudinal span: $7^{\circ} 33'$ |
| **Standard Time Meridian** | **$82^{\circ} 30'\text{ E}$ (IST)** passes through **Mirzapur (near Prayagraj)** | Calculates Indian Standard Time (GMT $+5:30$) |
| **Total Geographic Area** | **240,928 sq km** (7.33% of India's total area) | **4th Largest State** by area (Rajasthan > MP > Maharashtra > UP) |
| **East-to-West Length** | **650 km** | Extreme length |
| **North-to-South Width** | **240 km** | Narrower width |
| **Number of Neighboring Entities** | **8 States + 1 UT (Delhi) + 1 International (Nepal)** | Shares borders with the **maximum number of states** in India |

### Boundary Touch-Points Master Grid
- **Northern Boundary:** Uttarakhand & Himachal Pradesh (Himachal touches only **Saharanpur** - shortest border).
- **North-Eastern Boundary:** **Nepal** (Touches **7 Districts:** Pilibhit, Lakhimpur Kheri, Bahraich, Shrawasti, Balrampur, Siddharthnagar, Maharajganj — 579 km border).
- **Western Boundary:** Haryana, Delhi (UT), Rajasthan.
  - Touches Haryana: Saharanpur, Shamli, Baghpat, Gautam Buddha Nagar, Aligarh, Mathura (6 districts; separated by Yamuna river).
  - Touches Delhi: Ghaziabad & Gautam Buddha Nagar.
  - Touches Rajasthan: **Mathura & Agra** (2 districts).
- **Southern Boundary:** **Madhya Pradesh** (Touches **11 Districts:** Agra, Etawah, Jalaun, Jhansi, Lalitpur, Mahoba, Banda, Chitrakoot, Prayagraj, Mirzapur, Sonbhadra — longest inter-state border).
- **South-Eastern Boundary:** **Chhattisgarh** (Touches only **Sonbhadra**) & **Jharkhand** (Touches only **Sonbhadra**).
- **Eastern Boundary:** **Bihar** (Touches 7 Districts: Sonbhadra, Chandauli, Ghazipur, Ballia, Deoria, Kushinagar, Maharajganj).

> **Four District Extremes:**  
> - **Northernmost District:** **Saharanpur** (surrounded by Haryana, HP, Uttarakhand)  
> - **Southernmost District:** **Sonbhadra** (**Only district in India bordering 4 states**: MP, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, Bihar)  
> - **Easternmost District:** **Ballia** (wedged into Bihar)  
> - **Westernmost District:** **Shamli**  
> - **Largest District by Area:** **Lakhimpur Kheri** (7,680 sq km)  
> - **Smallest District by Area:** **Hapur** (660 sq km)

---

## 2. Drainage System, Rivers, Canals & Wetlands of UP

### A. Major Rivers of Uttar Pradesh
| River | Entry Point in UP | Exit Point / Confluence | Total Districts in UP & Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Ganga** | Enters at **Bijnor** | Exits UP at **Ballia** into Bihar | Traverses **28 districts** (~1,450 km in UP). Receives Ramganga at Kannauj, Yamuna at Prayagraj, Gomti at Saidpur (Ghazipur), Ghaghara at Chhapra (Bihar border). |
| **Yamuna** | Enters at **Saharanpur** (Faizabad village) | Joins Ganga at **Prayagraj (Triveni Sangam)** | Traverses **19 districts**. Receives Hindon at Noida, Chambal at Pachnada (Etawah), Betwa at Hamirpur, Ken at Chilla (Banda). |
| **Ramganga** | Enters at **Bijnor** (near Kalagarh) | Joins Ganga near **Kannauj** | Passes through Corbett National Park; flows via Moradabad, Bareilly. |
| **Gomti** | Originates at **Fulhar Lake (Panghaili Jheel), Pilibhit** | Joins Ganga near Saidpur, Ghazipur | **Only major Himalayan tributary originating in the plains of UP**. Lucknow, Sultanpur, Jaunpur situated on its banks. |
| **Ghaghara (Saryu)** | Enters near Kheri/Bahraich | Joins Ganga at Chhapra (Bihar) | Originates at Mapchachungo glacier (Tibet); called Karnali in Nepal, Saryu at Ayodhya. |
| **Betwa (Vetravati)** | Enters at **Lalitpur** | Joins Yamuna at **Hamirpur** | Originates in Vindhyan range (MP); Rajghat, Parichha, and Matatila dams built on it. |
| **Ken (Karnavati)** | Enters at **Banda** | Joins Yamuna at **Chilla (Banda)** | Ken-Betwa river interlinking project is India's first river link project. |
| **Son** | Enters and exits in **Sonbhadra** | Joins Ganga near Patna | Originates at Amarkantak plateau (MP). |

### B. Major Canals & Reservoirs
- **Sharda Canal:** Longest canal network in Uttar Pradesh (originates from Sharda river at Banbasa, India-Nepal border).
- **Upper Ganga Canal:** Constructed by Sir Proby Cautley (1854); originates at Haridwar (Bhimoda).
- **Govind Ballabh Pant Sagar:** **Largest artificial lake in India**, formed by the **Rihand Dam** on Rihand river at Pipri in **Sonbhadra**.
- **Parichha Dam & Matatila Dam:** Built on **Betwa river** (Jhansi / Lalitpur).
- **Keetham Lake (Sur Sarovar):** Ramsar site in **Agra**.

### C. All 10 Ramsar Wetland Sites in Uttar Pradesh
1. **Upper Ganga River (Brijghat to Narora Stretch):** First Ramsar site in UP (designated 2005).
2. **Nawabganj Bird Sanctuary** (Unnao) - renamed Shahid Chandra Shekhar Azad Bird Sanctuary.
3. **Parvati Arga Bird Sanctuary** (Gonda) - oxbow lake.
4. **Saman Bird Sanctuary** (Mainpuri).
5. **Samaspur Bird Sanctuary** (Rae Bareli).
6. **Sandi Bird Sanctuary** (Hardoi).
7. **Sarsai Nawar Jheel** (Etawah) - sarus crane habitat.
8. **Sur Sarovar (Keetham Lake)** (Agra).
9. **Haiderpur Wetland** (Muzaffarnagar & Bijnor, designated 2021).
10. **Bakhira Wildlife Sanctuary** (Sant Kabir Nagar, designated 2022).

---

## 3. National Parks, Tiger Reserves & Wildlife Sanctuaries

- **Only National Park in UP:** **Dudhwa National Park** (Lakhimpur Kheri, established 1977). Famous for Swamp Deer (Barasingha) and One-horned Rhinoceros reintroduction.
- **Tiger Reserves in UP (4 Total):**
  1. **Dudhwa Tiger Reserve** (Lakhimpur Kheri - declared 1987)
  2. **Pilibhit Tiger Reserve** (Pilibhit - declared 2014; won TX2 global award)
  3. **Amangarh Tiger Reserve** (Bijnor - buffer zone of Jim Corbett NP retained in UP after UK partition)
  4. **Ranipur Tiger Reserve** (**Chitrakoot** - declared 2022 as UP's 4th and India's 53rd Tiger Reserve, in Bundelkhand region).
- **Elephant Reserve:** Terai Elephant Reserve (TER) established in Lakhimpur Kheri/Pilibhit belt (2022).
- **Hastinapur Wildlife Sanctuary:** **Largest wildlife sanctuary in UP** (spread across Meerut, Muzzafarnagar, Bijnor, Amroha, Hapur).
- **Mahavir Swami Wildlife Sanctuary:** **Smallest wildlife sanctuary in UP** (Lalitpur, ~5.4 sq km).
- **Kachhua (Turtle) Sanctuary:** Varanasi (now shifted to Bhadohi/Prayagraj stretch).
- **State Symbols:** Bird: **Sarus Crane** (*Grus antigone*); Animal: **Swamp Deer** (Barasingha); Tree: **Ashoka**; Flower: **Palash / Dhak** (*Butea monosperma* - Flame of the Forest); Aquatic Animal: **Gangetic Dolphin** (*Platanista gangetica*).

---

## 4. 9 Agro-Climatic Zones & Agriculture of UP

1. **Tarai Zone:** Bhabhar and Terai belt along foothills (high rainfall, sugarcane, paddy).
2. **Western Plain Zone:** Meerut division, high agricultural productivity (Green Revolution heartland).
3. **Mid-Western Plain Zone:** Bareilly & Moradabad divisions.
4. **South-Western Semi-Arid Zone:** Agra, Mathura, Aligarh (low rainfall, mustard, bajra).
5. **Central Plain Zone:** Lucknow, Kanpur, Ayodhya divisions (alluvial doab).
6. **Bundelkhand Zone:** 7 districts (Jhansi, Lalitpur, Jalaun, Hamirpur, Mahoba, Banda, Chitrakoot) — semi-arid, rocky terrain, pulses, oilseeds.
7. **North-Eastern Plain Zone:** Gorakhpur division (floods, sugarcane, rice).
8. **Eastern Plain Zone:** Varanasi, Azamgarh, Ghazipur, Jaunpur divisions.
9. **Vindhyan Zone:** Mirzapur, Sonbhadra, and southern Chandauli (plateau, red soil, coarse grains).

> **UP Agricultural Ranks in India:**  
> - **Rank 1 in:** Total Foodgrain production, Wheat, Sugarcane, Potato, Milk, Mango, Guava, Ethanol production.  
> - **Rank 2 in:** Rice (after West Bengal).

---

## 5. Minerals & Industrial Belts in Uttar Pradesh

- Uttar Pradesh is generally poor in metallic minerals; mineral wealth is concentrated in the southern plateau districts (Bundelkhand & Vindhyan region):
  - **Uranium:** **Lalitpur** (only district in UP with Uranium occurrences)
  - **Copper:** **Sonrai area, Lalitpur**
  - **Limestone:** **Sonbhadra** (Gurma, Kanach, Kajrahat) & **Mirzapur** (supplies raw material for cement factories at Churk, Dalla, Chunar)
  - **Glass Sand (Silica Sand):** **Shankargarh & Lohgara (Prayagraj)**, Chitrakoot, Banda (derived from disintegration of Vindhyan sandstones)
  - **Bauxite (Aluminium ore):** **Banda, Chitrakoot, Chandauli** (supplies Renukoot Hindalco smelter)
  - **Coal:** **Singrauli (Sonbhadra)** — power capital of India (NTPC plants)
  - **Dolomite:** **Sonbhadra & Banda**
  - **Pyrophyllite & Diaspore:** **Jhansi, Lalitpur, Mahoba, Hamirpur**
  - **China Clay:** **Sonbhadra & Banda**
  - **Gold:** Small placer deposits in **Sharda and Ramganga river sands**; Hardi village in Sonbhadra.

| **Bareilly** | **Khan Bahadur Khan** | Vincent Eyre, Colin Campbell |
| **Faizabad (Ayodhya)**| **Maulvi Ahmadullah Shah** (*Danka Shah*) | Campbell |

## 7. Cultural Gharanas, Folk Music & Dances

- **Kathak:** Only Classical Dance of North India. Reached its zenith under **Nawab Wajid Ali Shah** of Awadh.
  - **Lucknow Gharana exponents:** Thakur Prasad, Kalka-Bindadin Maharaj, Achchan Maharaj, Shambhu Maharaj, Lachhu Maharaj, **Pt. Birju Maharaj**.
  - **Banaras Gharana exponents:** Sitara Devi, Pt. Kishan Maharaj (Tabla), Pt. Samta Prasad (Gudai Maharaj - Tabla), Bismillah Khan (Shehnai - Bharat Ratna 2001).
  - **Kirana Gharana:** Founded by **Ustad Abdul Karim Khan & Abdul Wahid Khan** in Muzaffarnagar district (Bhimsen Joshi, Gangubai Hangal belonged to this tradition).
  - **Atrali Gharana:** Aligarh.
- **Folk Music Genres:**
  - **Rasiya:** Braj region (Mathura, Vrindavan) — themed on Radha-Krishna romance.
  - **Kajri:** Mirzapur & Varanasi region — sung during monsoon / rainy season.
  - **Birha:** Eastern UP (Bhojpuri belt) — heroic and narrative folklore.
  - **Alha:** Bundelkhand region (Mahoba) — martial epic commemorating heroic deeds of Alha & Udal.
  - **Chaiti & Hori:** Purvanchal & Braj region.
- **Folk Dances of UP:**
  - **Charkula Dance:** **Braj region** — women balance a multi-tiered wooden pyramid with 108 oil lamps on their heads.
  - **Karma Dance:** **Sonbhadra & Mirzapur** (performed by tribal communities like Kharwar).
  - **Pai Danda & Diwari:** **Bundelkhand** (stick dance reminiscent of Raas Leela).
  - **Nautanki:** Traditional folk theatre of UP (Hathras & Kanpur schools).

## 1. Complete Vitamin Master Reference & Deficiency Diseases

| Vitamin | Chemical / Scientific Name | Fat / Water Soluble | Key Deficiency Disease / Symptoms | Primary Natural Food Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Vitamin A** | **Retinol** | Fat-soluble | **Night blindness (Nyctalopia)**, **Xerophthalmia** (dry cornea), Keratomalacia | Carrot, Papaya, Mango, Liver, Fish oil, Golden Rice |
| **Vitamin B1** | **Thiamine** | Water-soluble | **Beri-beri** (Peripheral neuropathy / cardiac failure), Wernicke-Korsakoff syndrome | Unpolished rice, yeast, whole grains (*lost in polished rice*) |
| **Vitamin B2** | **Riboflavin** | Water-soluble | **Cheilosis** (cracking at corners of mouth), Glossitis (red swollen tongue) | Milk, eggs, liver (*gives yellow colour to milk/whey*) |
| **Vitamin B3** | **Niacin (Nicotinic acid)** | Water-soluble | **Pellagra** (4Ds: Dermatitis, Diarrhea, Dementia, Death) | Yeast, meat, fish, legumes |
| **Vitamin B5** | **Pantothenic acid** | Water-soluble | Burning feet syndrome, fatigue | Mushroom, eggs, broccoli |
| **Vitamin B6** | **Pyridoxine** | Water-soluble | Peripheral neuropathy, microcytic anemia, convulsions in infants | Cereals, bananas, meat |
| **Vitamin B7** | **Biotin (Vitamin H)** | Water-soluble | Hair loss (alopecia), dermatitis | Egg yolk, nuts, intestinal flora (*avidin in raw egg binds biotin*) |
| **Vitamin B9** | **Folic Acid (Folate)** | Water-soluble | **Megaloblastic anemia**, Neural tube defects in fetuses (Spina bifida) | Green leafy vegetables, citrus fruits |
| **Vitamin B12** | **Cyanocobalamin** | Water-soluble | **Pernicious anemia**, demyelination of spinal cord (*contains **Cobalt** metal*) | Meat, fish, eggs, dairy (*Absent in plant foods*) |
| **Vitamin C** | **Ascorbic acid** | Water-soluble | **Scurvy** (bleeding gums, petechiae, impaired wound healing); heat-sensitive (*destroyed by cooking*) | Aonla (highest source), Guava, Citrus fruits (Lemon, Orange) |
| **Vitamin D** | **Calciferol** ($D_2$: Ergocalciferol, $D_3$: Cholecalciferol) | Fat-soluble | **Rickets** in children (bowed legs), **Osteomalacia** in adults (*hormone-like vitamin*) | Sunlight synthesis in skin, Cod liver oil |
| **Vitamin E** | **Tocopherol** | Fat-soluble | Infertility / reproductive failure, muscle weakness, hemolytic anemia (*Beauty Vitamin / Anti-oxidant*) | Wheat germ oil (richest), sunflower seeds, almonds |
| **Vitamin K** | **Phylloquinone** ($K_1$), Menaquinone ($K_2$) | Fat-soluble | **Defective blood clotting**, prolonged bleeding time (needed for synthesis of prothrombin in liver) | Spinach, kale, synthesized by human gut bacteria |

> **Fat-Soluble vs Water-Soluble Mnemonic:**  
> Fat-soluble: **K - E - D - A** (stored in liver and adipose tissue, hypervitaminosis possible).  
> Water-soluble: **B-complex and C** (excreted in urine, regular intake required).

## 2. Infectious Diseases & Causative Pathogens Master Matrix

### A. Viral Diseases
| Disease | Causative Virus | Mode of Transmission / Key Diagnostic Feature |
|---|---|---|
| **COVID-19** | SARS-CoV-2 (enveloped positive-sense RNA) | Respiratory droplets; RT-PCR test |
| **Rabies (Hydrophobia)** | Lyssavirus / Rhabdovirus (bullet-shaped RNA) | Bite of rabid dogs, bats; affects central nervous system |
| **Poliomyelitis** | Poliovirus (Enterovirus, single-stranded RNA) | Fecal-oral route; destroys anterior horn motor neurons of spinal cord; Oral Polio Vaccine (Sabin), Inactivated Polio Vaccine (Salk) |
| **AIDS** | Human Immunodeficiency Virus (HIV - Retrovirus with Reverse Transcriptase) | Blood, sexual, maternal; attacks **CD4+ Helper T-cells**; ELISA test, Western Blot confirmatory |
| **Dengue (Break-bone fever)** | Flavivirus (DEN-1 to DEN-4, ssRNA) | Transmitted by **Aedes aegypti** mosquito (day-biting); severe thrombocytopenia (platelet drop) |
| **Chikungunya** | Chikungunya virus (Alphavirus) | Transmitted by **Aedes aegypti** and *Aedes albopictus*; severe joint pain |
| **Yellow Fever** | Flavivirus | *Aedes aegypti* mosquito vector |
| **Zika Virus** | Flavivirus | *Aedes* mosquito; causes **Microcephaly** in newborns |
| **Hepatitis B** | Hepatitis B Virus (HBV - **DNA virus**, whereas Hep A, C, D, E are RNA) | Blood, body fluids; attacks liver; vaccine available |
| **Smallpox** | Variola virus (DNA virus) | Completely **eradicated from world in 1980** (WHO) |
| **Measles, Mumps, Rubella** | Morbillivirus, Paramyxovirus, Rubivirus | MMR vaccine |

### B. Bacterial Diseases
| Disease | Causative Bacterium | Key Clinical Feature / Diagnostic Test |
|---|---|---|
| **Tuberculosis (TB)** | *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* (acid-fast bacillus) | Affects lungs; **Mantoux test**; **BCG vaccine** (Bacillus Calmette-Guérin) |
| **Cholera** | *Vibrio cholerae* (comma-shaped) | *"Rice water stools"*, massive dehydration; fecal-oral route |
| **Typhoid (Enteric fever)** | *Salmonella typhi* | Step-ladder fever pattern, intestinal perforation; **Widal test** |
| **Tetanus (Lockjaw)** | *Clostridium tetani* (anaerobic, drumstick-shaped) | Neurotoxin tetanospasmin causes severe muscle spasms; ATS / Tetanus toxoid |
| **Diphtheria** | *Corynebacterium diphtheriae* | Grey pseudomembrane over throat / larynx; **Schick test** |
| **Pertussis (Whooping Cough)**| *Bordetella pertussis* | Paroxysmal coughing ending in whoop; DPT triple vaccine |
| **Plague (Black Death)** | *Yersinia pestis* | Transmitted by **Xenopsylla cheopis** (oriental rat flea); Bubonic, Pneumonic forms |
| **Leprosy (Hansen's Disease)** | *Mycobacterium leprae* | Hypopigmented skin patches with loss of sensation; Lepromin test |
| **Anthrax** | *Bacillus anthracis* (spore-forming) | Zoonotic disease of cattle; potential bioweapon |
| **Syphilis & Gonorrhea** | *Treponema pallidum* & *Neisseria gonorrhoeae* | Sexually transmitted bacterial infections |

### C. Protozoan & Other Pathogen Diseases
- **Malaria:** Caused by protozoan **Plasmodium** (*P. vivax, P. falciparum* [deadliest malignant tertian], *P. malariae, P. ovale*). Vector: **Female Anopheles mosquito**. Infective stage to humans: **Sporozoite**. Parasite multiplies in liver cells and destroys red blood cells (**RBCs**), releasing **hemozoin toxin** causing periodic shivering chills and high fever. Discovered by **Sir Ronald Ross** (in Secunderabad, India - Nobel Prize 1902). Treatment: Quinine (extracted from **Cinchona bark**), Artemisinin.
- **Kala-Azar (Visceral Leishmaniasis / Dum-Dum Fever):** Caused by *Leishmania donovani*. Vector: **Phlebotomus sandfly**. Enlarges spleen and liver (hepatosplenomegaly).
- **Sleeping Sickness (African Trypanosomiasis):** Caused by *Trypanosoma brucei*. Vector: **Tsetse fly** (*Glossina*).
- **Amoebic Dysentery:** Caused by *Entamoeba histolytica*.
- **Ringworm (Tinea):** Fungal infection (*Trichophyton, Microsporum, Epidermophyton*).
- **Filariasis / Elephantiasis:** Caused by parasitic nematode worm *Wuchereria bancrofti*. Transmitted by **Culex mosquito**. Blocks lymphatic vessels causing swelling of limbs/scrotum.

## 3. Human Physiology & Endocrine Hormones

| Gland | Hormones Produced | Primary Function & Prelims Exam Triggers |
|---|---|---|
| **Pituitary (Master Gland)** | Growth Hormone (GH), TSH, ACTH, FSH, LH, Prolactin, **Oxytocin**, **Vasopressin (ADH)** | Regulated by Hypothalamus. Hypersecretion of GH causes **Gigantism / Acromegaly**; hyposecretion causes **Dwarfism**. **ADH (Antidiuretic Hormone)** controls water reabsorption in kidneys; deficiency causes **Diabetes Insipidus** (frequent urination without sugar). **Oxytocin** is the milk-ejection & birth hormone. |
| **Thyroid** | **Thyroxine ($T_4$)**, Triiodothyronine ($T_3$), Calcitonin | Largest endocrine gland; requires **Iodine**. Deficiency causes **Simple Goitre** (enlarged neck), **Cretinism** in infants, **Myxedema** in adults. Hyperthyroidism causes **Grave's Disease** (exophthalmic goitre). |
| **Parathyroid** | Parathyroid Hormone (PTH / Collip's hormone) | **Increases blood calcium levels** (hypercalcemic) by dissolving bone; acts antagonistically to thyroid calcitonin. |
| **Pancreas (Islets of Langerhans)**| **Insulin** (from $\beta$-cells), **Glucagon** (from $\alpha$-cells), Somatostatin (from $\delta$-cells) | **Insulin lowers blood glucose** by converting glucose to glycogen (glycogenesis). Deficiency causes **Diabetes Mellitus** (hyperglycemia, glycosuria). Discovered by Banting & Best (1921). Glucagon raises blood glucose. |
| **Adrenal Gland** | Adrenaline (Epinephrine) & Noradrenaline; Cortisol, Aldosterone | Adrenaline is the **"Emergency Hormone" / "Fight, Flight or Fright" (3F/4S hormone)**; increases heart rate, breathing, blood pressure. |
| **Pineal Gland** | **Melatonin** | Regulates circadian rhythm (24-hour sleep-wake cycle). |

> **Blood Groups & ABO System:** Discovered by **Karl Landsteiner** (1900).  
> - **Universal Donor:** **$O^-$ (O Negative)** — has neither A nor B antigens and no Rh factor on RBCs.  
> - **Universal Recipient:** **$AB^+$ (AB Positive)** — has both A and B antigens and Rh factor; has no anti-A or anti-B antibodies in plasma.  
> - **Normal Blood Pressure:** **120/80 mm Hg** (Systolic/Diastolic), measured by **Sphygmomanometer**. Blood pH: **7.35–7.45 (slightly basic)**.

---

## 4. Optics & Physics Principles (Key Phenomena & Eye Defects)

| Phenomenon | Underlying Optical Principle | Exam Application / Examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Mirage in deserts / Looming in cold** | **Total Internal Reflection (TIR)** | Optical illusion; light travels from denser to rarer medium at angle $>$ critical angle. |
| **Sparkling of Diamond** | **Total Internal Reflection (TIR)** | Very small critical angle of diamond ($pprox 24.4^{\circ}$) due to high refractive index ($pprox 2.42$). |
| **Endoscope / Optical Fibre Cables** | **Total Internal Reflection (TIR)** | Zero signal loss communication; invented by Narinder Singh Kapany. |
| **Blue colour of sky / Red colour of sunset** | **Rayleigh Scattering of Light** | Scattering $\propto \frac{1}{\lambda^4}$. Blue light (shorter wavelength) scatters most; red light (longest wavelength) scatters least and reaches eyes at horizon. |
| **Twinkling of Stars** | **Atmospheric Refraction** | Continuously fluctuating refractive index of atmospheric air layers. |
| **Coin appears raised / Stick bent in water** | **Refraction of Light** | Light bends away from normal when entering air from water. |
| **Rainbow Formation** | **Dispersion $+$ Refraction $+$ Total Internal Reflection** | Water droplets act as tiny prisms. Primary rainbow: 2 refractions $+$ 1 internal reflection; Secondary rainbow: 2 refractions $+$ 2 internal reflections. |
| **Soap bubble shining with brilliant colours** | **Interference of Light** | Destructive and constructive interference of light reflected from upper and lower thin film surfaces. |

### Common Eye Defects & Corrective Lenses
1. **Myopia (Short-sightedness):** Nearby objects seen clearly, distant objects blurred. Image formed **in front of retina**. Corrected by **Concave Lens** (diverging lens).
2. **Hypermetropia (Long-sightedness):** Distant objects seen clearly, nearby objects blurred. Image formed **behind retina**. Corrected by **Convex Lens** (converging lens).
3. **Presbyopia (Old age vision):** Loss of accommodation power of ciliary muscles. Corrected by **Bifocal Lens** (upper part concave for distance, lower part convex for reading).
4. **Astigmatism:** Inability to focus simultaneously on horizontal and vertical lines due to irregular cornea curvature. Corrected by **Cylindrical Lens**.

## 5. Commercial Names of Common Chemical Compounds

| **Baking Soda** | $\text{NaHCO}_3$ | Sodium hydrogen carbonate | Baking, antacid, fire extinguisher |

| **Bleaching Powder** | $\text{CaOCl}_2$ | Calcium hypochlorite | Disinfectant for water, bleaching cotton |
| **Quicklime** | $\text{CaO}$ | Calcium oxide | Cement manufacture, white washing |
| **Slaked Lime** | $\text{Ca(OH)}_2$ | Calcium hydroxide | Mortar, neutralizing soil acidity |
| **Plaster of Paris (POP)** | $\text{CaSO}_4 \cdot \frac{1}{2}\text{H}_2\text{O}$ | Calcium sulphate hemihydrate | Bone fractures, statues, false ceilings |
| **Gypsum** | $\text{CaSO}_4 \cdot 2\text{H}_2\text{O}$ | Calcium sulphate dihydrate | Retarder in cement setting, soil reclamation |

| **Heavy Water** | $\text{D}_2\text{O}$ | Deuterium oxide | Moderator in nuclear reactors |
| **Dry Ice** | $\text{Solid CO}_2$ | Solid carbon dioxide | Refrigerant, sublimes without melting |
| **Epsom Salt** | $\text{MgSO}_4 \cdot 7\text{H}_2\text{O}$ | Magnesium sulphate heptahydrate | Laxative, bath salt |
| **Blue Vitriol** | $\text{CuSO}_4 \cdot 5\text{H}_2\text{O}$ | Copper sulphate pentahydrate | Fungicide (Bordeaux mixture), electroplating |
| **Green Vitriol** | $\text{FeSO}_4 \cdot 7\text{H}_2\text{O}$ | Ferrous sulphate heptahydrate | Ink manufacturing |
| **White Vitriol** | $\text{ZnSO}_4 \cdot 7\text{H}_2\text{O}$ | Zinc sulphate heptahydrate | Eye drops, pigments |

## 6. Major Alloys & Their Compositions

| Alloy | Major Constituents (%) | Primary Properties & Applications |
|---|---|---|
| **Brass (Pital)** | **Copper (60–70%) $+$ Zinc (30–40%)** | Utensils, musical instruments, cartridge cases (*No Tin*) |
| **Bronze (Kansa)** | **Copper (88%) $+$ Tin (12%)** | Statues, medals, coins, bells (*No Zinc*) |
| **German Silver** | **Copper (50%) $+$ Zinc (30%) $+$ Nickel (20%)** | **Contains ZERO Silver!** Utensils, artificial jewellery |
| **Stainless Steel** | **Iron $+$ Chromium (18%) $+$ Nickel (8%) $+$ Carbon (0.1–1%)** | Rust-resistant; Chromium gives corrosion resistance |
| **Solder (Tanka)** | **Lead (50%) $+$ Tin (50%)** | Low melting point; joining electrical wires |
| **Duralumin** | **Aluminium (95%) $+$ Copper (4%) $+$ Magnesium (0.5%) $+$ Manganese (0.5%)** | Light and strong; aircraft bodies, pressure cookers |
| **Gun Metal** | **Copper (88%) $+$ Tin (10%) $+$ Zinc (2%)** | Heavy machinery gears, gun barrels |
| **Bell Metal** | **Copper (78%) $+$ Tin (22%)** | High resonance for casting bells |
| **Amalgam** | **Mercury $+$ Any other metal** (except Iron, Platinum, Tungsten) | Dental fillings (Silver-Mercury amalgam); **Iron does not form amalgam** |
| **Nichrome** | **Nickel (60%) $+$ Chromium (40%)** | High resistance & high melting point; electric heater coils |

---

## 7. Indian Space Programme (ISRO) & Defence Tech

- **ISRO Founded:** **15 August 1969**; Headquarters: **Bengaluru**; Space Commission & Department of Space (DoS) formed in 1972.
- **Launch Port:** Satish Dhawan Space Centre (SDSC - SHAR), **Sriharikota (Andhra Pradesh)**, on Pulicat Lake barrier island. Second spaceport under construction at **Kulasekarapattinam (Tamil Nadu)** for SSLV launches.
- **Key Historic Missions:**
  - **Aryabhata:** India's first satellite, launched on **19 April 1975** by Soviet Kosmos-3M rocket.
  - **Chandrayaan-1:** Launched on **22 October 2008** via PSLV-C11; Moon Impact Probe detected water molecules ($H_2O/OH$) on lunar surface.
  - **Mangalyaan (Mars Orbiter Mission - MOM):** Launched on **5 November 2013** via PSLV-C25; inserted into Martian orbit on **24 September 2014** on maiden attempt.
  - **Chandrayaan-3:** Launched on **14 July 2023** via LVM3-M4; successfully achieved historic **soft landing near lunar South Pole** on **23 August 2023** (celebrated as **National Space Day**). Lander: *Vikram*, Rover: *Pragyan*. Landing site named **Shiv Shakti Point**.
  - **Aditya-L1:** India's first solar observatory launched on **2 September 2023** via PSLV-C57; inserted into halo orbit around **Lagrange Point 1 (L1)** on 6 January 2024.
- **DRDO Missiles (Integrated Guided Missile Development Programme - IGMDP, 1983 - Dr. APJ Abdul Kalam):**
  - **P - Prithvi:** Surface-to-Surface short-range ballistic missile (Liquid fuel).
  - **A - Agni:** Surface-to-Surface medium-to-intercontinental ballistic missile (Agni I to V, Solid fuel, MIRV tech).
  - **T - Trishul:** Surface-to-Air short-range missile.
  - **N - Nag:** Anti-Tank guided missile (*"Fire and Forget"*); helicopter-launched version is **Helina / Dhruvastra**.
  - **A - Akash:** Surface-to-Air medium-range missile with Rajendra multi-target phased array radar.
- **Nuclear Power Plants in India:**
  1. **Narora Atomic Power Station (NAPS):** **Bulandshahr, Uttar Pradesh** (PHWR, only nuclear plant in UP)
  2. **Tarapur:** Maharashtra (Oldest nuclear plant in India, 1969)
  3. **Rawatbhata:** Rajasthan (near Kota, Chambal river)
  4. **Kakrapar:** Gujarat (Tapi river)
  5. **Kaiga:** Karnataka (Kali river)
  6. **Kalpakkam (MAPS):** Tamil Nadu (Indira Gandhi Centre for Atomic Research)
  7. **Kudankulam:** Tamil Nadu (Largest nuclear power capacity in India, VVER Russian reactors)
  8. **Gorakhpur Nuclear Plant:** Under construction in **Fatehabad, Haryana** (Not in Gorakhpur, UP!)
